MySQL has no percentile function, so `p50UpperBoundMs` / `p95UpperBoundMs` are **histogram upper bounds**: the smallest bound that 50 % / 95 % of the samples do not exceed ("p95 ≤ 5 s"), exact for the histogram, never an estimate; `null` without samples or when the percentile lies above the last bound ("> 15 min"). `averageMs` is the mean, rounded to whole milliseconds (a negative clock skew counts as 0).

**Top attacked domains (step 7.1).** Inbound messages (`direction = inbound`) of the window per domain of the organization, with the threat verdicts `spam, phishing, malware, bec, suspicious` (`threatVerdicts`). Only domains with at least one threat are listed, ordered by `threats` and `inbound` (descending), then `name`; at most `limit`. Each entry carries the public `domainId`, `name`, `status` and `deleted` (a domain deleted after its mail arrived is still listed, with `deleted: true`). Messages without a tenant domain are counted in `unattributed`. A message that references a domain outside the organization is never shown: the report answers `503 inconsistent_read`.

```bash
curl -s "$API/api/v1/reports/top-domains?limit=5" -H "X-API-Key: $API_KEY"
# → {"threatVerdicts":["spam","phishing","malware","bec","suspicious"],
#    "domains":[{"domainId":"0193…","name":"alpha.example.test","status":"active","deleted":false,"inbound":812,"threats":37,
#                "verdicts":{"spam":30,"phishing":3,"malware":1,"bec":0,"suspicious":3}}],
#    "unattributed":{"inbound":4,"threats":1},…}
```

### Usage and billing (`usage`, `billing` tags, step 6.1)

| Method | Path                    | Permission             | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------ | ----------------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET    | `/usage`                | `organization:read`, K | Exactly `GET /organizations/{id}/usage` for the caller's organization (the key's, the user's, or the override target): users, domains, messages by direction, active quarantine entries, API keys, sessions, limits and their use. Default 30 days, ≤ 31 days                                 |
| GET    | `/billing/plan`         | `billing:read`, K      | `{ plan: { code, name, description }, limits: [{ key, value, source: plan \| override }], features: [{ key, enabled, source }], pricing: null, note }`. No prices, invoices or payments exist                                                                                                 |
| GET    | `/billing/usage?month=` | `billing:read`, K      | `month=YYYY-MM` (default the current UTC month, counted up to now): messages by direction, current users, domains, active API keys, limits. Future months or months older than the message retention (6 months + the current one) → `400 usage_period_unavailable`. Cached; class `expensive` |

### Threat-intelligence lookup (`threat-intelligence` tag, step 6.1)

| Method | Path                          | Permission             | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------ | ----------------------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET    | `/threat-intelligence/lookup` | `threat_intel:read`, K | `type=sha256\|url\|domain\|ip`, `value`. Normalized exactly as the engines do; matched by `value_sha256` against the platform indicators the URL engine and click-service use (`org_scope = 0`, not expired; a domain also matches its parent domains down to the registrable domain) and, for `sha256`, the known-threat hash catalogue. `{ type, valueSha256, known, matches: [{ source, severity, category, firstSeenAt, lastSeenAt, expiresAt }] }`; never analyst reasons or user ids. Class `expensive`; audited `threat_intel.looked_up` (the value as SHA-256) |

```bash
curl -s "$API/api/v1/threat-intelligence/lookup?type=domain&value=login.evil-example.test" -H "X-API-Key: $API_KEY"
# → {"type":"domain","valueSha256":"…","known":true,"matches":[{"source":"platform_indicator","severity":"high","category":"phishing",…}]}
```

Tenant-managed indicators are not offered: no engine reads tenant-owned `threat_indicators` rows, so managing them would change nothing (step 6.1 §1.2). Your own allow-lists are BEC trusted senders, DLP exceptions and policy conditions.

### Webhooks (`webhooks` tag, step 6.2)

Signed HTTP callbacks for mail, threat, quarantine and domain events of your organization. Design: [docs/plan/steps/19-webhooks-notifications.md](../plan/steps/19-webhooks-notifications.md); decisions: [ADR-0054](../architecture/adr/0054-webhook-event-model.md) (event model), [ADR-0055](../architecture/adr/0055-webhook-signing-and-delivery.md) (signing and delivery), [ADR-0057](../architecture/adr/0057-idempotent-secret-bearing-routes-redacted-replay.md) (redacted replay of the secret routes). Reads need `webhook:read`, every change `webhook:manage`; API keys may hold both. Creating, re-activating, testing and replaying need the plan feature `webhooks` (`403 webhooks_not_enabled`; on in the standard, enterprise and dedicated plans, off in trial). Another organization's webhook, delivery or replay id answers `404` like a missing one.

| Method | Path                                                   | Permission          | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET    | `/webhooks`                                            | `webhook:read`, K   | Newest first, v1 cursor; `status` (≤ 4; default every status except `deleted`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| POST   | `/webhooks`                                            | `webhook:manage`, K | **Idempotency-Key required** (redacted replay), class `sensitive`. `{ name, description?, url, eventTypes (1–10), filters?, status?: active \| paused, maxConcurrency? (1–32) }` → `201` with the webhook and `secret` (shown once). `409 webhook_limit_reached` (WEBHOOK_MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_ORG, default 10 live webhooks), `422 webhook_url_not_allowed` (`reason`), `422 webhook_filter_invalid` (`path`). Audit `webhook.created`                                         |
| GET    | `/webhooks/event-types`                                | `webhook:read`, K   | The catalogue: type, version, description, severity rule, applicable filters, example envelope                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| GET    | `/webhooks/{webhookId}`                                | `webhook:read`, K   | `404 webhook_not_found`. Deleted webhooks are shown with `status: deleted`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| PATCH  | `/webhooks/{webhookId}`                                | `webhook:manage`, K | Any of `name`, `description`, `url`, `eventTypes`, `filters`, `status` (`active`, `paused`, `disabled`), `maxConcurrency`, with `expectedRowVersion` (`409 webhook_version_conflict` with `currentRowVersion`). `paused` parks the pending deliveries (`nextAttemptAt: null`, still `pending`); `active` makes them due now; `disabled` cancels them. Audit `webhook.updated` (changed paths; `urlHost` and `urlSha256` for a new URL; `parkedDeliveries` / `resumedDeliveries`) |
| DELETE | `/webhooks/{webhookId}`                                | `webhook:manage`, K | **Idempotency-Key required**, `sensitive`. Soft delete; pending deliveries are cancelled in the same transaction → `204` (a replay answers `204` again; a new key is `404`). Audit `webhook.deleted`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| POST   | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/rotate-secret`                  | `webhook:manage`, K | **Idempotency-Key required** (redacted replay), `sensitive`. `{ overlapHours? (0–168, default 24) }` → `200 { id, secret, secretVersion, secretRotatedAt, previousSecretExpiresAt }`. Audit `webhook.secret_rotated` (version and overlap only)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| POST   | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/test`                           | `webhook:manage`, K | A `webhook.ping` event and a `trigger: test` delivery → `202 { deliveryId, eventId }`. Webhook must be `active` (`409 webhook_not_active`). RL: WEBHOOK_TEST_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE (6) per webhook. Audit `webhook.test_requested`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| GET    | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/deliveries`                     | `webhook:read`, K   | History newest event first, v1 cursor; required range on the event time `from`/`to` (default the last 24 h, at most WEBHOOK_HISTORY_MAX_RANGE_DAYS = 31); filters `status` (≤ 5), `eventType` (≤ 10), `trigger` (`event`, `replay`, `test`); scan-bounded when filtered. Kept 2 months                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| GET    | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/deliveries/{deliveryId}`        | `webhook:read`, K   | The delivery with every attempt (outcome, HTTP status, latency, sanitized response snippet ≤ 512 characters, Retry-After, error code, signature count) and the stored envelope (`null` after retention). `404 webhook_delivery_not_found`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| POST   | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/deliveries/{deliveryId}/replay` | `webhook:manage`, K | **Idempotency-Key required**, `sensitive`. A new delivery of the same stored event → `202 { deliveryId }`. `409 webhook_not_active`, `409 webhook_replay_limit` (WEBHOOK_REPLAY_MAX_PER_DELIVERY = 10 per event), `410 webhook_event_expired`. Audit `webhook.delivery_replayed`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| POST   | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/replays`                        | `webhook:manage`, K | **Idempotency-Key required**, `sensitive`. `{ from, to (≤ 72 h, within retention), statuses? (default failed, dead; ⊆ succeeded, failed, dead, cancelled), eventTypes? }` → `202 { replayId, status: queued }`. One queued or running replay per webhook (`409 webhook_replay_in_progress` with `replayId`). RL: WEBHOOK_REPLAY_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR (10) per organization. Audit `webhook.replay_requested`                                                                        |
| GET    | `/webhooks/{webhookId}/replays/{replayId}`             | `webhook:read`, K   | `{ id, status, from, to, statuses, eventTypes, matchedCount, createdCount, truncated, errorCode, createdAt, startedAt, completedAt }`. `404 webhook_replay_not_found`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Webhook representation:** `{ id, name, description, url, eventTypes, filters, status, disabledReason, disabledAt, maxConcurrency, secretVersion, secretRotatedAt, previousSecretExpiresAt, consecutiveFailures, failingSince, lastSuccessAt, lastFailureAt, createdBy: { type: "user", id } | null, createdAt, updatedAt, rowVersion }`. The secret and its ciphertext are never returned again. The `url` is shown to `webhook:read` holders (it may carry a receiver token): `webhook:read` is therefore not part of the read-only role.

**Event types and filters.** A webhook lists 1–14 types of the catalogue below (10 since step 6.2, 4 added by step 8.4) (no wildcard; `webhook.ping` is sent only by the test route). Optional `filters` (strict; every field optional): `directions` (1–3 of `inbound`, `outbound`, `internal`), `domainIds` (1–20 of **your** domain ids; another organization's or an unknown id is `422 webhook_filter_invalid`), `minSeverity` (`info` < `low` < `medium` < `high`), `verdicts` (1–7 scan verdicts). An event is delivered when its type is listed **and** every present filter matches the attribute the event carries (domain events carry no direction or verdict and pass those filters; a message of no tenant domain never matches `domainIds`). Only events that occur after the webhook was created, while it is `active`, are delivered: there is no back-fill. Changes reach the delivery worker within its cache TTL (5 s by default).

**Endpoint URL rules** (checked at create and update, `422 webhook_url_not_allowed` with `reason`):

- absolute `https` URL of at most 2048 characters, without userinfo (`user:pass@`), fragment (`#…`), whitespace or control characters (`reason: format`, or `scheme` for another scheme; plain `http` only where the TEST-ONLY `WEBHOOK_ALLOW_HTTP` is set, which the configuration refuses outside local/CI);
- a port of `WEBHOOK_ALLOWED_PORTS` (default 443; `reason: port`);
- a host that is not `localhost`, a single label or a `.local`/`.internal`/`.localdomain`/`home.arpa` name (`name`), not the click-service host, the portal host or the host the API request reached (`deny_list`), and, for an IP literal, not a loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast, documentation, metadata (169.254.169.254, fd00:ec2::254), ULA, IPv4-mapped or other reserved address (the reason names the class).

DNS is **not** resolved at registration (it can change). The delivery worker resolves the host at every attempt and refuses the attempt when **any** answer is a non-public address (DNS rebinding), dials the checked address itself, re-checks the connected address and never follows redirects. A refused attempt fails with `lastError: blocked:<reason>` and is not retried.

**Secrets, shown once, and the redacted replay (ADR-0057).** Create and rotate return the signing secret (`whsec_` followed by 43 base64url characters; 256 bits) in that response only. It is stored encrypted (AES-256-GCM, bound to your organization, the webhook and the secret version) with a key only the api (encrypt) and the delivery worker (decrypt) hold; it is never logged, audited or stored in an idempotency record. Both routes require an `Idempotency-Key`: the stored response is the same body with `secret: null`, so repeating the request with the same key (within 24 h) returns `Idempotency-Replayed: true` and `secret: null` and creates or rotates nothing. **If you lost the original response, rotate the secret with a new key.**

**Rotation overlap.** `rotate-secret` makes a new secret current (`secretVersion + 1`); the retiring secret keeps signing until `previousSecretExpiresAt` (`overlapHours`, default 24, 0 = none). During the overlap every request carries two `v1=` signatures (the new secret first), so a receiver can deploy the new secret at its own pace. Rotating again during an overlap retires the then-current secret (only one previous secret exists).

**The delivery contract.** The worker sends `POST <url>` with the stored envelope as body (UTF-8 JSON, at most 16 KiB, byte-identical on every attempt and replay) and exactly these headers:

| Header                | Value                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Content-Type`        | `application/json`                                                                                                            |
| `User-Agent`          | `EmailGateway-Webhooks/1`                                                                                                     |
| `EG-Signature`        | `t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(secret, "<t>.<raw body>")>`; a second `,v1=…` during a rotation overlap (current first) |
| `EG-Event-Id`         | the envelope `id` (deterministic: the same event always has the same id)                                                      |
| `EG-Event-Type`       | the envelope `type`                                                                                                           |
| `EG-Delivery-Id`      | the delivery id (new for each replay)                                                                                         |
| `EG-Delivery-Attempt` | attempt number, 1-based                                                                                                       |
| `EG-Subscription-Id`  | the webhook id                                                                                                                |
| `Idempotency-Key`     | the envelope `id`                                                                                                             |

The HMAC key is the UTF-8 bytes of the **whole** secret string (including `whsec_`). Receivers must: **verify before parsing** (over the raw body bytes), reject a timestamp more than **300 s** away from their clock, compare signatures in **constant time** and accept the request when **any** `v1` matches any of their current secrets; answer `2xx` within 10 s (process asynchronously); **deduplicate by `EG-Event-Id`** (delivery is at least once, and replays re-send the same event).

**Retries, Retry-After, dead letters, auto-disable.** `2xx` is delivered. Every other status, redirects (`3xx` is never followed), timeouts (3 s connect, 10 s total), DNS, connection and TLS errors and oversized responses are retried with exponential backoff and jitter (about 0 s, 30 s, 1.5 min, 3.5 min, 7.5 min, 15.5 min, 31.5 min, 63.5 min: 8 attempts by default). A `Retry-After` on any non-`2xx` answer (seconds or HTTP date) is honoured up to 1 h (a larger value is clamped). After the last attempt, or 24 h after the first, the delivery becomes `dead`: dead deliveries are the webhook dead-letter store (list them with `status=dead`, replay them). A webhook whose deliveries fail continuously (by default 50 consecutive failures over at least 24 h) is disabled by the system (`disabledReason: sustained_failure`, audited `webhook.auto_disabled`, a security alert to `webhook:manage` holders); re-enable it with `PATCH { status: "active" }` and replay what you missed. While a webhook is `paused` no new deliveries are created and pending ones wait; `disabled` and deleted webhooks have their pending deliveries cancelled.

**Replay semantics.** A replay (single or range) re-sends the **stored** envelope: same `EG-Event-Id` and bytes, a new `EG-Delivery-Id`, timestamp and signature. Events that occurred while a webhook was paused or disabled were never recorded for it and cannot be replayed. A range replay skips events that already have `WEBHOOK_REPLAY_MAX_PER_DELIVERY` (default 10) replays, so `createdCount` can be lower than `matchedCount`; it ends `failed` (`errorCode`) when the webhook is no longer active (`webhook_not_active`), the organization is suspended or being deleted (`organization_inactive`) or the `webhooks` feature was revoked (`webhooks_not_enabled`). While an organization is not active or lacks the feature, nothing is delivered: its due deliveries become `cancelled` (`lastError` `organization_inactive` / `webhooks_not_enabled`) and can be replayed afterwards. Stored events and the delivery history are kept 2 months (`410 webhook_event_expired` afterwards).

**What payloads never contain.** No message body, subject, attachment content or file name, address local part (domains only), raw header other than `internetMessageId`, URL found in mail, S3 key, secret or token. A payload guard refuses to build an envelope that would violate this. Response snippets shown in the history are untrusted text (secrets and tokens redacted): never render them as HTML.

#### Verifying a delivery (Node.js, `node:crypto` only)

<!-- webhook-verify-snippet:start -->

```js
import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';

/**
 * Verifies an EmailGateway webhook request. `rawBody` is the exact request body (a Buffer, read
 * BEFORE any JSON parsing); `header` is the EG-Signature header; `secrets` are your current
 * secrets (two during a rotation). Returns true when the timestamp is within the tolerance and
 * any v1 signature matches any secret.
 */
export function verifyEmailGatewayWebhook(
  rawBody,
  header,
  secrets,
  toleranceSeconds = 300,
  nowMs = Date.now(),
) {
  if (typeof header !== 'string' || header.length > 1024) return false;
  let timestamp = null;
  const signatures = [];
  for (const part of header.split(',')) {
    const eq = part.indexOf('=');
    if (eq < 1) return false;
    const key = part.slice(0, eq).trim();
    const value = part.slice(eq + 1).trim();
    if (key === 't') timestamp = value;
    else if (key === 'v1' && /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(value))
      signatures.push(Buffer.from(value, 'hex'));
  }
  if (timestamp === null || !/^\d{1,12}$/.test(timestamp) || signatures.length === 0) return false;
  if (Math.abs(Math.floor(nowMs / 1000) - Number(timestamp)) > toleranceSeconds) return false;
  let valid = false;
  for (const secret of secrets) {
    const expected = createHmac('sha256', Buffer.from(secret, 'utf8'))
      .update(`${timestamp}.`)
      .update(rawBody)
      .digest();
    // No early exit: every comparison runs in constant time.
    for (const signature of signatures) {
      if (timingSafeEqual(expected, signature)) valid = true;
    }
  }
  return valid;
}
```

<!-- webhook-verify-snippet:end -->

With Express, read the raw body (`express.raw({ type: 'application/json', limit: '64kb' })`), call `verifyEmailGatewayWebhook(req.body, req.get('EG-Signature'), [process.env.EG_WEBHOOK_SECRET])` and answer `401` when it returns false; only then `JSON.parse(req.body)`, skip events whose `EG-Event-Id` you already processed, and answer `204`. `apps/api/src/modules/webhooks/verify-snippet.test.ts` runs this exact snippet against the platform's signer (valid, tampered body, wrong secret, stale and future timestamps, rotation with two `v1`).

#### Verifying or producing a signature with openssl and curl

```bash
SECRET='whsec_…'                                  # your webhook secret
BODY_FILE=delivery.json                           # the raw body exactly as received
T=1763025243                                      # the t= value of EG-Signature
# The expected v1 value: compare it with the header's v1 (any of them).
printf '%s.' "$T" | cat - "$BODY_FILE" | openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$SECRET" -hex | sed 's/^.*= //'

# Exercise your receiver with a correctly signed request (current time):
T=$(date +%s); SIG=$(printf '%s.' "$T" | cat - "$BODY_FILE" | openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$SECRET" -hex | sed 's/^.*= //')
curl -sS -X POST "https://hooks.example.com/email-gateway" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H "EG-Signature: t=$T,v1=$SIG" -H "EG-Event-Id: $(jq -r .id "$BODY_FILE")" --data-binary @"$BODY_FILE"
```

```bash
# Register a webhook (the secret is in this response only), then send it a test event.
curl -s -X POST "$API/api/v1/webhooks" -H "X-API-Key: $API_KEY" -H "Idempotency-Key: hook-siem-0001" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"name":"SIEM","url":"https://hooks.example.com/email-gateway","eventTypes":["email.threat.detected","email.quarantined"],"filters":{"minSeverity":"medium"}}'
# → 201 {"id":"0193…","status":"active","secretVersion":1,…,"secret":"whsec_…"}
curl -s -X POST "$API/api/v1/webhooks/0193…/test" -H "X-API-Key: $API_KEY"
# → 202 {"deliveryId":"0193…","eventId":"…"}
```

#### Event catalogue (payload version 1)

Every envelope is `{ id, type, version, occurredAt, organizationId, data }`: `id` is deterministic (the same event redelivered or replayed keeps it), `occurredAt` is when the fact happened, `organizationId` is your organization's public id. A breaking change of a type's `data` would ship as `version: 2` without changing version 1 (not built in 6.2). Shared objects: `message` = `{ id, receivedAt, direction, domainId | null, internetMessageId | null, senderDomain | null, headerFromDomain | null, recipientCount, recipientDomains (≤ 20), sizeBytes, attachmentCount }` (domains only, never local parts or a subject); `domain` = `{ id, name, status }` (your own domain). Severities: `info` < `low` < `medium` < `high`.

**`email.received`** — A message was accepted and stored by the gateway. Severity: info. Filters: `directions`, `domainIds`, `minSeverity`.

```json
{
  "id": "89ce8a67-39df-8ab9-a503-4ae5c0b19b77",
  "type": "email.received",
  "version": 1,
  "occurredAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.120Z",
  "organizationId": "0192f0c4-7d1e-7b6a-9c55-6f3e2a1d4b10",
  "data": {
    "message": {
      "id": "0192f0c4-8a11-7c2e-9d3f-1a2b3c4d5e6f",
      "receivedAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:02.481Z",
      "direction": "inbound",
      "domainId": "0192f0c4-7d1e-7b6a-9c55-6f3e2a1d4b11",
      "internetMessageId": "<CAF1x2y3z@mail.partner.example>",
      "senderDomain": "partner.example",
      "headerFromDomain": "partner.example",
      "recipientCount": 2,
      "recipientDomains": ["acme.example"],
      "sizeBytes": 48213,
      "attachmentCount": 1
    }
  }
}
```

**`email.scanned`** — A scan round of a message completed (initial scan or rescan). Severity: clean/unknown/timeout/error → info, spam → low, suspicious → medium, malicious → high. Filters: `directions`, `domainIds`, `minSeverity`, `verdicts`.

```json
{
  "id": "9e83e129-eb3d-8e4c-8cc1-55b055ed602d",
  "type": "email.scanned",
  "version": 1,
  "occurredAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.120Z",
  "organizationId": "0192f0c4-7d1e-7b6a-9c55-6f3e2a1d4b10",
  "data": {
    "message": "<the message object, as in email.received>",
    "scan": {
      "lifecycleRound": 0,
      "kind": "initial",
      "outcome": "scanned",
      "verdict": "malicious",
      "engines": [
        {
          "engine": "rspamd",
          "outcome": "suspicious"
        },
        {
          "engine": "attachment",
          "outcome": "malicious"
        },
        {
          "engine": "url",
          "outcome": "clean"
        }
      ],
      "reasonCodes": ["attachment.clamav_match", "spam.score"],
      "completedAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.010Z"
    }
  }
}
```

**`email.threat.detected`** — The final verdict of a message is spam, suspicious or malicious, or the sandbox found a malicious attachment after delivery. Severity: spam → low, suspicious → medium, malicious → high. Filters: `directions`, `domainIds`, `minSeverity`, `verdicts`.

```json
{
  "id": "8754c726-2d7b-80f4-a78d-9cfb9653d672",
  "type": "email.threat.detected",
  "version": 1,
  "occurredAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.120Z",
  "organizationId": "0192f0c4-7d1e-7b6a-9c55-6f3e2a1d4b10",
  "data": {
    "message": "<the message object, as in email.received>",
    "detection": {
      "stage": "scan",
      "verdict": "malicious",
      "riskScore": 940,
      "reasonCodes": ["attachment.clamav_match"],
      "lifecycleRound": 0,
      "finalAction": "quarantine",
      "attachment": null
    }
  }
}
```

**`email.policy.triggered`** — At least one tenant policy rule matched a message. Severity: reject/quarantine/require_approval dispositions → medium, otherwise low. Filters: `directions`, `domainIds`, `minSeverity`.

```json
{
  "id": "ac346d1b-045e-8624-bc51-dd103bd44770",
  "type": "email.policy.triggered",
  "version": 1,
  "occurredAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.120Z",
  "organizationId": "0192f0c4-7d1e-7b6a-9c55-6f3e2a1d4b10",
  "data": {
    "message": "<the message object, as in email.received>",
    "policy": {
      "disposition": "quarantine",
      "finalAction": "quarantine",
      "decisionSource": "tenant_rule",
      "decisive": {

        }

      "actions": ["ALERT", "TAG"],
      "overrideApplied": false,
      "lifecycleRound": 0,
      "evaluatedAt": "2026-11-13T09:14:03.120Z"
    }
  }
}
```

    }

**Step 8.4 types (security incidents and account takeover).** Metadata only: public ids, codes, levels and times; never an address, an IP address or a name. Severity: incident severity / risk level critical and high → `high`, medium → `medium`, low → `low` (the exact level is in `data`); `ato.response.completed` failures → `medium`, otherwise `info`. Filter: `minSeverity` only. **Receivers filter incidents by `data.incident.category` and must ignore unknown categories** (advanced threat protection categories arrive later under the same two types).

| Type                        | Source event                   | `data`                                                                                                                   |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `security.incident.created` | `security.incident.created.v1` | `{ incident: { id, category, status: "open", severity, origin, detectedAt, reasonCodes }, subject: { type, id } }`       |
| `security.incident.updated` | `security.incident.updated.v1` | `{ incident: { id, category, status, severity }, subject: { type, id }, previous: { status, severity }, changedFields }` |
| `ato.account.risk_changed`  | `ato.account.risk.updated.v1`  | `{ account: { id, kind }, risk: { score, level, previousLevel } }` (sent only when the level changes)                    |
| `ato.response.completed`    | `ato.response.completed.v1`    | `{ response: { id, type, status, provider, providerStatus }, incident: { id } \| null, account: { id, kind } }`          |

Not events (by design, step 6.2 §11): SMTP-time refusals (no message is stored), per-recipient delivery outcomes (`email.delivered` carries counts; per-recipient detail is `GET /messages/{id}/recipients`), URL clicks, DLP incidents, archive and connector events (8.1, 8.2), quarantine adoption of pre-5.3 messages.

### Notifications (`notifications` tag, step 6.2)

Security alerts by e-mail ([ADR-0056](../architecture/adr/0056-security-alert-notifications.md)): **threat detected** (a threat at or above the configured severity), **DNS record failures** (a verified domain's verification, MX, SPF, DKIM or DMARC record stops validating) and **webhook disabled** (sustained delivery failure). They go to the active, verified users of your organization holding the kind's permission (`quarantine:approve`, `domain:manage`, `webhook:manage`) who did not opt out, plus `organization_settings.notification_email` when `includeOrganizationContact` is on; at most one alert per organization, kind, scope (the domain, the webhook) and throttle window, the next one saying how many events it summarizes. Alerts carry ids, counts, the verdict and links into the portal: never a subject, address, URL from mail, file name, endpoint URL or secret. The existing policy alerts (5.2) and quarantine admin alerts, approval requests and digests (5.3) are unchanged and honour the same preference center.

| Method | Path                         | Permission             | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------ | ---------------------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET    | `/notifications/settings`    | `organization:read`, K | `{ settings (effective), stored (or null), invalidStored, rowVersion (0 = nothing stored), updatedAt, defaults, bounds }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| PUT    | `/notifications/settings`    | `settings:manage`, K   | `{ settings, expectedRowVersion }` replaces the whole document (`{}` = defaults). `400 notification_settings_invalid` with `violations` (path, rule), `409 notification_settings_conflict` with `currentRowVersion`. Audit `notification.settings_updated` (changed paths only)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| GET    | `/notifications/preferences` | authenticated, **U**   | Your own: `{ optOut, quarantineDigestOptOut, quarantineDigestOptOutAllowed, kinds, invalidStored }`. API keys and platform overrides get `403`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| PUT    | `/notifications/preferences` | authenticated, **U**   | `{ optOut: kinds (unique), quarantineDigestOptOut }`. Kinds: `threat_detected`, `domain_dns_failed`, `webhook_disabled`, `policy_alert`, `quarantine_admin_alert`, `quarantine_approval_requested`; step 8.4: `ato_incident` (account takeover incident alerts and response failures), `ato_approval_requested`, `ato_digest`; step 8.5: `ato_provider_health` (an identity-provider integration became unhealthy). The digest opt-out is the same setting as `/quarantine/me/preferences` and needs the organization's `digest.allowOptOut` (`400` otherwise). Audit `notification.preferences_updated` |

Settings document (strict, every key optional; defaults in brackets): `{ alerts: { threatDetected: { enabled [true], minSeverity: medium | high [high], throttleMinutes: 15–1440 [60] }, domainDnsFailed: { enabled [true], throttleMinutes: 60–10080 [360] }, webhookDisabled: { enabled [true], throttleMinutes: 60–10080 [1440] }, includeOrganizationContact [true] } }`. Decisions on your own release requests and sender notices are not optional. SMS, chat and push channels are not offered.

```bash
curl -s -X PUT "$API/api/v1/notifications/settings" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"settings":{"alerts":{"threatDetected":{"minSeverity":"medium","throttleMinutes":30}}},"expectedRowVersion":0}'
# → 200 {"settings":{"alerts":{"threatDetected":{"enabled":true,"minSeverity":"medium","throttleMinutes":30},…}},"rowVersion":1,…}
```

### Saved views (`saved-views` tag, step 7.2)

A user's saved Email Explorer views ([ADR-0061](../architecture/adr/0061-message-search-drivers-and-saved-views.md)): per user and organization, kind `message_explorer`, read from the **writer** (a view is read right after it is saved). `message:read`; **users who are members of the organization only**: API keys get `403 user_required`, a platform override session `403 saved_views_member_only`. Another user's or organization's view is `404 saved_view_not_found`.

| Method | Path                    | Permission        | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------ | ----------------------- | ----------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GET    | `/saved-views`          | `message:read`, U | `kind=message_explorer` (required), `limit`, `cursor`, `sort=-createdAt` (newest first, v1 cursor)                                                                                                                            |
| POST   | `/saved-views`          | `message:read`, U | `{ kind, name, definition }` → `201`. `409 saved_view_name_taken` (case-insensitive, per user and kind), `409 saved_view_limit_reached` (`SAVED_VIEWS_MAX_PER_USER`, 50); filter rules as below. Audited `saved_view.created` |
| GET    | `/saved-views/{viewId}` | `message:read`, U | The view                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| PUT    | `/saved-views/{viewId}` | `message:read`, U | `{ name, definition, expectedRowVersion }` → the view with `rowVersion + 1`; `409 saved_view_conflict` (`currentRowVersion`) or `409 saved_view_name_taken`. Audited `saved_view.updated`                                     |
| DELETE | `/saved-views/{viewId}` | `message:read`, U | `204`. Audited `saved_view.deleted`                                                                                                                                                                                           |

`definition` is strict: `{ version: 1, filters: { "<GET /messages parameter>": "<value>" }, sort: "-receivedAt" | "receivedAt", range: { kind: "relative", preset: "1h" | "24h" | "7d" | "30d" } | { kind: "absolute", from, to }, columns: string[] | null }` with at most 24 filters (not `limit`, `cursor`, `sort`, `from`, `to`), values of at most 998 characters, at most 24 unique column ids and at most 8 192 bytes of JSON. The filters are validated with the same rules as `GET /messages` (`400 validation_failed` with paths under `definition`, e.g. `definition.filters.subjectPrefix: …`) and its filter permissions (`403 message_filter_forbidden`). A view keeps the filter values its owner typed (they may include an address or a subject): they are never logged and the audit rows hold only the view id and kind.

```bash
curl -s -X POST "$API/api/v1/saved-views" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"kind":"message_explorer","name":"Inbound phishing, last 7 days","definition":{"version":1,"filters":{"verdict":"phishing,malware","direction":"inbound"},"sort":"-receivedAt","range":{"kind":"relative","preset":"7d"},"columns":null}}'
# → 201 {"id":"0193…","kind":"message_explorer","name":"Inbound phishing, last 7 days","definition":{…},"rowVersion":1,…}
```

### Journaling (`journaling` tag, step 8.6)

Step 8.6 splits the former archive area in two (design [27-journal-intake-and-archive-split.md](../plan/steps/27-journal-intake-and-archive-split.md) D1, D8, D10–D12; ADR-0087, ADR-0089): **Journaling** is capture — what is journaled (the capture half of the archive settings) and journal-only intake from Microsoft 365 / on-premises Exchange journal reports, Google Workspace copies and SMTP BCC copies; **Archive** (below) is storage and use. OpenAPI groups them with `x-tagGroups` (`Journaling` = [`journaling`]; `Archive` = [`archive`, `ediscovery`]); the archive settings and capture-issue operations carry both tags. Permissions `journal:read` (in the read set) and `journal:manage` (organization admins, domain admins). **No breaking change**: every 8.1 path, operationId and response field is unchanged; additions only (the permission re-scope of four archive record routes is a deliberate access change, below).

| #   | Method | Path                                                   | operationId                          | Permission / guards                      | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| J1  | GET    | `/journaling/settings`                                 | `JournalingController_get_v1`        | `journal:read` or `archive:read`, K      | `enabled`, `entitled` (`archiving`), `intakeEntitled` (`journal_intake`), `intakeAvailable` (platform intake domain), `journalEnabledAt`, `settings.{directions,include,scope,policyArchive,intake}`, `generation`, `rowVersion` (shared with `/archive/settings`) |
| J2  | PUT    | `/journaling/settings`                                 | `JournalingController_update_v1`     | `journal:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive` | `{ expectedRowVersion, enabled?, settings?: { directions, include, scope, policyArchive, intake } }`: replaces the journaling sections of the same row; `access` is kept; 8.1 A22 rules; audited `archive.settings_updated` with `area: journaling`                |
| J3  | GET    | `/journaling/status`                                   | `JournalingController_status_v1`     | `journal:read`, K                        | capture intents by status, intake sources by status and health, today's intake counters                                                                                                                                                                            |
| J4  | GET    | `/journaling/intake-sources`                           | `IntakeSourcesController_list_v1`    | `journal:read`, K                        | v1 list (`journal_intake_sources`), `?status&kind&health`, newest first; addresses masked                                                                                                                                                                          |
| J5  | POST   | `/journaling/intake-sources`                           | `IntakeSourcesController_create_v1`  | `journal:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive` | `{ kind, displayName, providerConnectionId?, networks?, requireTls?, clientCertFingerprints?, maxMessageBytes?, silenceAlertMinutes? }` → `201 { source, address, headerToken }`; redacted replay (ADR-0057)                                                       |
| J6  | GET    | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}`                | `IntakeSourcesController_get_v1`     | `journal:read`, K                        | source, health, counters of the last 7 UTC days; `?reveal=true` (needs `journal:manage`, audited `journal_intake.address_revealed`) shows the address and token in clear                                                                                           |
| J7  | PATCH  | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}`                | `IntakeSourcesController_update_v1`  | `journal:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive` | `{ expectedRowVersion, displayName?, networks?, requireTls?, clientCertFingerprints?, maxMessageBytes?, silenceAlertMinutes?, status?: paused \| active }` (CAS `409 journal_intake_source_conflict`)                                                              |
| J8  | DELETE | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}`                | `IntakeSourcesController_disable_v1` | `journal:manage`, MFA, `sensitive`       | sets `disabled` (terminal; a repeat answers the disabled source)                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| J9  | POST   | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}/rotate-address` | `IntakeSourcesController_rotate_v1`  | `journal:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive` | new address (and header token) once; the previous ones stay valid `JOURNAL_INTAKE_ROTATION_OVERLAP_DAYS`; redacted replay                                                                                                                                          |
| J10 | GET    | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}/wizard`         | `IntakeSourcesController_wizard_v1`  | `journal:read`, K                        | computed steps `archiving`, `domains`, `connection`, `configure`, `verify`, `monitor` with blockers and `nextAction`                                                                                                                                               |
| J11 | GET    | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}/setup-guide`    | `IntakeSourcesController_guide_v1`   | `journal:read`, K                        | `journal-intake-guide/1` JSON, or Markdown with `?format=markdown` or `Accept: text/markdown`; address in clear only for `journal:manage` (audited, `via: guide`)                                                                                                  |
| J12 | POST   | `/journaling/intake-sources/{sourceId}/attestations`   | `IntakeSourcesController_attest_v1`  | `journal:manage`, MFA, `sensitive`       | `{ code, attested }` with a `journal.*` code of the kind (`422 journal_intake_attestation_unknown`); ≤ 32 kept                                                                                                                                                     |

**Sources and addresses (D1).** Kinds: `microsoft_365_journal` and `google_workspace_copy` need `providerConnectionId` — a step 8.2 connection of your organization, of the same provider, not disabled, with a bound tenant (`422 journal_intake_connection_invalid`, `reason`: `not_found`, `provider_mismatch`, `disabled`, `tenant_not_bound`); `exchange_onprem_journal` and `smtp_bcc` need `networks` (1–32 public CIDRs; never overlapping an active provider egress range, no prefix shorter than /16 IPv4 or /32 IPv6, no host bits, no duplicates; private ranges only in local/CI: `422 journal_intake_networks_invalid` with `issues: [{ code, index }]`, values never echoed). Kind-foreign fields are `400`. Creating or resuming needs the plan features `archiving` and `journal_intake` (`403 journal_intake_not_enabled`; a feature without an organization row falls back to the plan default), archiving enabled with a live default retention policy (`409 archiving_not_enabled`) and a TXT-verified domain (provider kinds: linked to the connection; `409 journal_intake_domain_required`, `reason`). At most `JOURNAL_INTAKE_MAX_SOURCES_PER_ORG` sources that are not disabled (`409 journal_intake_source_limit`, `limit`); unique names (`409 journal_intake_source_name_taken`); `maxMessageBytes` ≤ `JOURNAL_INTAKE_MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` (`400`). The address `journal-<token>@<JOURNAL_INTAKE_DOMAIN>` (and the Google `X-EG-Journal-Token` value) is derived from `JOURNAL_INTAKE_ADDRESS_SECRET`, the source id and its address version; only SHA-256 hashes are stored. Views show `journal-••••<last 4>@…` and `••••<last 4>`; the clear values appear in the live creation and rotation responses (the stored Idempotency-Key response carries `null`: a replay is redacted, ADR-0057), with `?reveal=true` and in the guide for `journal:manage` holders, every time audited. Without an intake domain on the platform intake is off: creation, rotation and the guide answer `503 journal_intake_unavailable` and views show `address: null`. A disabled source cannot change (`409 journal_intake_source_disabled`). Audit actions `journal_intake.source_created|updated|paused|resumed|disabled|address_rotated|address_revealed|attested` (resource `journal_intake_source`, ids and codes only). Intake mail is archived only: it never appears in the Email Explorer, message routes (`404 message_not_found`), reports or the gateway usage counts (`GET /organizations/{id}/usage` reports it as `messages.journalIntakeToday`).

```bash
# Create a Microsoft 365 journal source (MFA session; the address is in this response only).
curl -sS -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Idempotency-Key: $(uuidgen)" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' "$API/api/v1/journaling/intake-sources" \
  -d '{"kind":"microsoft_365_journal","displayName":"Contoso Microsoft 365","providerConnectionId":"<connection id>"}'
# → 201 {"source":{"id":"0193…","status":"pending_verification","address":"journal-••••wxyz@journal.example.com",…},
#        "address":"journal-<26 characters>@journal.example.com","headerToken":null}
```

### Archive (`archive` tag, step 8.1)

Journaling and compliance archiving (design [23-journaling-archiving.md](../plan/steps/23-journaling-archiving.md) §4.4, ADR-0064–0069). The archive-worker captures mail into encrypted, per-tenant archive copies (S3, optional Object Lock) and indexes metadata into OpenSearch; this API configures archiving, searches the metadata, reads archived content (always audited) and disposes of reviewed records. The api never writes archive objects or the index. **K** = API keys allowed (scopes); **MFA** = users with an MFA session only (`403 mfa_required`, API keys `403 user_required`); **Idem** = `Idempotency-Key` required (transactional; replay answers the stored response with `Idempotency-Replayed: true`, a different request under the same key `422 idempotency_key_reused`).

| #   | Method | Path                                            | Permission / guards                                                           | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| --- | ------ | ----------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | GET    | `/archive/settings`                             | `archive:read` or `journal:read`, K                                           | Effective `archive-settings/1` (defaults applied), `enabled`, `entitled` (plan feature `archiving`), `immutability`, `objectLockAvailable`, `defaultRetentionPolicyId`, `generation`, `journalEnabledAt`, `rowVersion` (0 = no row yet)                                                         |
| 2   | PUT    | `/archive/settings`                             | `journal:manage` or `archive:manage` + per path, MFA, Idem, `sensitive`       | `{ expectedRowVersion, enabled?, immutability?, defaultRetentionPolicyId?, settings? }` (CAS → `409 archive_settings_conflict` + `currentRowVersion`); rules below                                                                                                                              |
| 3   | GET    | `/archive/retention-policies`                   | `archive:read`, K                                                             | v1 list (`archive_retention_policies`), `priority` order                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 4   | POST   | `/archive/retention-policies`                   | `archive:manage`, K, Idem, `sensitive`                                        | `{ name, retentionDays, onExpiry: delete \| review, priority, criteria: archive-retention/1 }` → `201`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 5   | GET    | `/archive/retention-policies/{policyId}`        | `archive:read`, K                                                             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 6   | PUT    | `/archive/retention-policies/{policyId}`        | `archive:manage`, K, Idem, `sensitive`                                        | full replacement with `expectedRowVersion` (`409 archive_retention_policy_conflict`)                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 7   | DELETE | `/archive/retention-policies/{policyId}`        | `archive:manage`, K, Idem, `sensitive`                                        | soft delete → `204`; the default policy `409 archive_retention_policy_is_default`; the name becomes free again                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 8   | GET    | `/archive/keys`                                 | `archive:read`, K                                                             | v1 list (`archive_keys`) of tenant key versions: status, root key id, times, rotating user. Never key material                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 9   | POST   | `/archive/keys/rotate`                          | `archive:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive`                                      | `{ reason }` → `201 { key, previousVersion }`; the new version is active for new captures, the previous one decrypt-only                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 10  | GET    | `/archive/status`                               | `archive:read` or `journal:read`, K                                           | counts of your organization: intents, records, index queue and lag, disposals, keys, Object Lock and search availability                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 11  | GET    | `/archive/capture-issues`                       | `journal:manage` or `archive:manage`, K                                       | v1 list (`archive_capture_issues`): `failed`, `lost`, `skipped` with code `max_age` (aged out, never archived) and `pending` older than 6 h (waits in quarantine excluded); `?status`                                                                                                           |
| 11b | GET    | `/archive/capture-issues/{messageId}`           | `journal:manage` or `archive:manage`, K                                       | step AI-5: the capture intent of one message in the list item's view, any status (`pending`, `captured_open`, `closed`, `skipped`, `lost`, `failed`; replica); `404 archive_capture_issue_not_found` for an unknown message, one without an intent or another organization's; non-uuid id `400` |
| 12  | POST   | `/archive/capture-issues/{messageId}/retry`     | `journal:manage` or `archive:manage`, K, Idem, `sensitive`                    | `failed` → `pending` (`409 archive_capture_retry_not_allowed` otherwise, `lost` included)                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 13  | GET    | `/archive/disposals`                            | `archive:read`, K                                                             | v1 list (`archive_disposals`) of the disposal log; `?from&to` (≤ 366 days, default 30), `reason`, `state`                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 14  | GET    | `/archive/export-signing-keys`                  | `archive:read`, K                                                             | Ed25519 public keys (SPKI DER, base64url) of export manifests, current first                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 15  | GET    | `/archive/messages`                             | `archive:search`, K, `expensive`                                              | metadata search (below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 16  | GET    | `/archive/messages/{messageId}`                 | `archive:search`, K (8.6; was `archive:read`)                                 | detail from MySQL (replica): masked metadata and parties, capture, retention (stored and effective), matching holds (hold permissions only), immutability, integrity, rounds; audited `archive.message_viewed`                                                                                  |
| 17  | GET    | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/journal`         | `archive:search`, K (8.6; was `archive:read`)                                 | the decrypted journal report (envelope, header safe subset, attachments, parse outcome, deliveries); audited `archive.journal_viewed`                                                                                                                                                           |
| 18  | GET    | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/security`        | `archive:search`, K (8.6; was `archive:read`)                                 | every linked scan round from its frozen snapshot, mapped like `GET /messages/{id}/scans`; `dlpEvidence: not_retained`; audited `archive.security_viewed`                                                                                                                                        |
| 19  | POST   | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/reveal`          | `archive:search` + `message:reveal`, K, route limit (8.6; was `archive:read`) | `{ fields: [subject, recipients, headers] }` → clear values; audited `archive.revealed` before the response; never idempotency-stored                                                                                                                                                           |
| 20  | GET    | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/preview`         | `archive:retrieve`, MFA, route limit                                          | sanitized `preview/1` JSON; tenant switch `access.previewEnabled`; customer-only; audited `archive.previewed` before decryption                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 21  | POST   | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/download-grants` | `archive:retrieve`, MFA, re-auth, route limit                                 | `{ password, totpCode? }` → `201 { token, expiresAt, filename, sizeBytes, sha256 }`; tenant switch `access.downloadEnabled`; malicious records `409 archive_download_blocked`; customer-only                                                                                                    |
| 22  | GET    | `/archive/downloads/{token}`                    | `archive:retrieve` or `archive:export`, users                                 | single use; streams the decrypted EML (or an eDiscovery export ZIP) as an attachment                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 23  | POST   | `/archive/messages/{messageId}/dispose`         | `archive:manage`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive`                                      | `{ reason }` → `202`; only `expiry_review` records; retention and holds re-checked on the writer                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Step 8.6 changes (ADR-0089; additions only).** (1) **Access change:** the record routes #16–#19 need `archive:search` instead of `archive:read` — `read_only_user`, `platform_admin` and custom roles with `archive:read` but without `archive:search` lose archived-record detail (they never had search); `archive:read` is configuration and status only. Organization admins hold no archive access by default; the new `compliance_officer` role does, and the step 8.6 migration gave every existing organization admin that role (audited `rbac.archive_access_migrated`). (2) `PUT /archive/settings` authorizes each **changed path**: `enabled`, `settings.directions|include|scope|policyArchive|intake` need `journal:manage`; `immutability`, `defaultRetentionPolicyId`, `settings.access` need `archive:manage`; otherwise `403 archive_settings_forbidden_path` with `paths: [{ path, permission }]` (an omitted section of the replacing `settings` document whose effective value changes counts as a change). The audit row adds `area: archive` and `settingsPaths`. (3) `settings.intake.scanEnabled` (default false): scan journal intake messages received afterwards. (4) Search: `ingestSource` (`gateway`, `journal_intake`; records indexed before 8.6 count as `gateway`); the 8.1 `source` filter keeps its meaning (capture source `journal`/`policy`). (5) List items and detail gain `ingestSources`; detail gains `intake` (the intake source of an intake record, else null) and `mergedCopies[]` (`id`, `receivedAt`, `source`, `sizeBytes`, `sha256`, `contentVariant`, `recipientsAdded`, `mergedAt`: copies of the same message merged into the record, ADR-0088); parties recipients may carry `field` and `ingestSources` and a `null` direction (an external recipient of an external intake sender). (6) The journal view gains `intake` (the journal envelope with fields — BCC included — and expansions, addresses masked; the sender proof codes) and recipients' `field`. (7) `POST …/download-grants` accepts `variant` (a `mergedCopies[].id` with `contentVariant: true`; `404 archive_variant_not_found`) and the download streams that stored variant, decrypted under the record's key and verified against its own SHA-256 (filename `message-<id>.variant-<copy id>.eml`).

**Settings rules (A22, A10).** Enabling needs the plan feature `archiving` (`403 archiving_not_enabled`) and a live default retention policy (`422 archive_default_retention_required`); journaling starts with mail received after the enablement (`journalEnabledAt`, no backfill). Defaults of an enabled tenant: every direction journaled, rejected/quarantined/failed mail not included, scope `all`, policy ARCHIVE honoured, preview on, original download off. Scope domain, department and user ids must exist in your organization (`422 archive_scope_reference_invalid` with `path`, e.g. `settings.scope.domainIds.0`). `immutability`: `compliance` is never weakened (`409 archive_immutability_downgrade_forbidden`); a new governance/compliance mode needs S3 Object Lock (`409 archive_object_lock_unavailable`; see `objectLockAvailable`); a stronger mode is applied to existing records by the archive-worker. Every change increments `generation`. The other archive mutations — retention policies, capture retries, cases, searches, review and exports — need the plan feature as well; settings, keys, holds and disposal do not (A20).

**Retention (A11).** `retentionDays` 1 … min(plan limit `archive_retention_days`, `ARCHIVE_MAX_RETENTION_DAYS`) (`422 archive_retention_exceeds_limit` with `limit`); at most `ARCHIVE_RETENTION_POLICIES_MAX_PER_ORG` live policies (`422 archive_retention_policy_limit`); unique live names (`409 archive_retention_policy_name_taken`). Criteria (`archive-retention/1`): `directions`, `categories`, `sources`, `policyRuleRefs`, `domainIds`, `departmentIds` (ids of your organization). Changes apply to new captures; a longer policy also protects existing records at their stored date (lazy, extend-only); a shorter one never shortens a record.

**Search (#15, A17).** `?from&to` required (ISO with offset; ≤ `ARCHIVE_SEARCH_MAX_RANGE_DAYS`, `400 archive_search_range_invalid` with `maxDays`), filters `direction`, `category`, `source`, `status`, `sender`, `senderDomain`, `recipient`¹, `recipientDomain`, `subject`¹, `custodian`¹, `internetMessageId`, `hasAttachments`, `attachmentName`, `attachmentSha256` (≤ 10), `verdict`, `finalAction`, `riskScore` (`.gte/.lte`), `malicious`, `legalHold`, `holdId`², `sort` (`-receivedAt` \| `receivedAt`), `limit` 1–100, `cursor` (v1 `archive_messages`, bound to the filters, the range and the sort). ¹ needs `message:reveal`, ² needs `archive:legal_hold` or `ediscovery:manage`: otherwise `403 archive_filter_forbidden` (`filter`) before any query. No totals. Response header **`X-EG-Index-Lag-Seconds`**: the age of your oldest change not yet indexed (0 = up to date). The search runs only through your tenant alias with your organization's term filter. OpenSearch unavailable or `ARCHIVE_SEARCH_URL` unset: `503 archive_search_unavailable` (only this route; detail and content keep working).

**Masking and reveal (ADR-0062).** Every archive view masks subjects (`subject: null`, `subjectMasked: true`) and recipient addresses (`a***@example.com`) for every caller, API keys included; the sender side, the Message-ID and attachment names stay in clear. The journal shows the header safe subset in clear and To/Cc/Subject (and unknown names) as `{ value: null, masked: true }`. Clear values only through `POST /archive/messages/{id}/reveal` (`archive:read` + `message:reveal`, `ARCHIVE_REVEAL_PER_MINUTE` per caller), which commits `archive.revealed` (fields and counts) before responding.

**Content access (A19).** Journal, security, reveal, preview and downloads re-read the record on the **writer** before decrypting: a record being disposed is `410 archive_content_unavailable` even while the replica still shows it. The preview and every download grant are customer-only (`403 archive_override_forbidden` under a platform override) and MFA-only. Grants re-authorize (password, TOTP when enrolled; `401 quarantine_reauth_failed`, the 5.3 code; failures count toward the login lockout), are limited to `ARCHIVE_DOWNLOAD_GRANTS_PER_HOUR` per user (message and export grants together) and return a token bound to your organization, you, the kind and the target, valid `ARCHIVE_DOWNLOAD_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`. `GET /archive/downloads/{token}`: once (`410 archive_download_token_used`), by the same user (`404 archive_download_token_invalid` otherwise), audit row committed before streaming with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="message-<id>.eml"` (exports `ediscovery-export-<id>.zip`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`. The plaintext SHA-256 is verified while streaming; on a mismatch the transfer is aborted (an incomplete body; `archive_integrity_failures_total` alerts). Per-attachment downloads are not offered (the original contains them).

**Disposal (#23, A13, A14).** Records whose retention ended with `onExpiry: review` wait in `status: expiry_review` (`GET /archive/messages?status=expiry_review`). Disposal re-checks the live policy, a running Object Lock and every active legal hold on the writer with the record locked (`409 archive_dispose_not_allowed`, `reason: retention | legal_hold | <status>`); otherwise the record's data key is destroyed in the same transaction (crypto-shredding), the disposal is logged (`GET /archive/disposals`) and the archive-worker deletes the object versions (and replica copies), the index document and the row.

```bash
# Last 30 days of inbound mail with attachments, masked; the lag header tells index freshness.
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  "$API/api/v1/archive/messages?from=2026-10-18T00:00:00Z&to=2026-11-17T00:00:00Z&direction=inbound&hasAttachments=true" -D -
# → X-EG-Index-Lag-Seconds: 2
# → {"data":[{"messageId":"0193…","subject":null,"subjectMasked":true,"recipients":["a***@corp.example"],…}],"nextCursor":"…"}
```

### Legal holds and eDiscovery (`ediscovery` tag, step 8.1)

| #   | Method | Path                                                            | Permission / guards                                          | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 24  | GET    | `/legal-holds`                                                  | `archive:legal_hold` or `ediscovery:manage`, K               | v1 list (`legal_holds`), `?status&caseId`; custodians are confidential (`archive:read` alone is not enough)                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 25  | GET    | `/legal-holds/{holdId}`                                         | `archive:legal_hold` or `ediscovery:manage`, K               |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 26  | POST   | `/legal-holds/{holdId}/release`                                 | `archive:legal_hold`, MFA, Idem, `sensitive`                 | `{ reason }`; `409 legal_hold_already_released`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 27  | POST   | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/holds`                              | `archive:legal_hold` + `ediscovery:manage`, MFA, Idem        | `{ name, reason, criteria?: archive-hold/1, useCaseCustodians? }` → `201`; open case only; ≤ `ARCHIVE_HOLDS_MAX_ACTIVE_PER_ORG` active (`422 legal_hold_limit_reached`); `422 legal_hold_criteria_invalid` (`reason`); explicit custodians need `message:reveal` (`403 archive_filter_forbidden`) |
| 28  | GET    | `/ediscovery/cases`                                             | `ediscovery:manage`, K                                       | v1 list (`ediscovery_cases`), `?status`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

| 30  | GET    | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}`                                    | `ediscovery:manage`, K                                       | custodians (with the matching user) and counts                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

| 33  | POST   | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/searches`                           | `ediscovery:manage` + `archive:search`, K, Idem, `expensive` | `{ criteria: archive-search/1 }` → `202 queued`; reveal-gated criteria need `message:reveal`; range ≤ `ARCHIVE_CASE_SEARCH_MAX_RANGE_DAYS`; ≤ `ARCHIVE_CASE_MAX_ACTIVE_SEARCHES` queued/running per case (`429 ediscovery_search_limit`)                                                          |

| 35  | GET    | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/searches/{searchId}`                | `ediscovery:manage`, K                                       | status, `hitCount`, `truncated`, `indexComplete`, `incompleteReason`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 36  | GET    | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/searches/{searchId}/hits`           | `ediscovery:manage`, K                                       | v1 list (`ediscovery_search_hits`), `?reviewTag`; masked records; first page audited                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 37  | POST   | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/searches/{searchId}/review`         | `ediscovery:manage`, users, Idem, `sensitive`                | `{ tag, messageIds }` (≤ `ARCHIVE_REVIEW_MAX_ITEMS`, `400 ediscovery_review_limit`); completed searches only                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 38  | POST   | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/exports`                            | `ediscovery:manage` + `archive:export`, K, Idem, `expensive` | `{ searchId, reviewTags?, acknowledgeMalicious? }` → `202 queued`; `422 ediscovery_export_too_large` (`limit`, `unit`), `409 ediscovery_export_malicious_ack_required` (`maliciousCount`)                                                                                                         |
| 39  | GET    | `/ediscovery/cases/{caseId}/exports`                            | `ediscovery:manage`, K                                       | v1 list (`ediscovery_exports`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
