Same conventions as API-1/API-2: tenant from the credential (another organization's ids `404`), strict bodies, every write keyed (transactional Idempotency-Key, rate class `sensitive`) and audited in the same transaction (ids, counts, codes and hashes; never subjects, bodies or criteria values beyond counts), signed tenant-bound v1 cursors, no MFA- or user-only route (approve and deny refuse API keys themselves). **No breaking change**: additions only.

| #    | Method | Path                                                  | operationId                                     | Permission / guards                              | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---- | ------ | ----------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| AC1  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks`                                 | `ApiModeClawbackController_create_v1`           | `remediation:purge`, Idem                        | `{ criteria?: { messageId?, attachmentSha256?, url?, urlSha256?, sender?, subject?, from?, to? }, seedDetectionId?, expand?, directions?, reason }` → `202 { id, status: searching, trigger, rowVersion }`; `422 api_mode_clawback_criteria_invalid` (`codes`)        |
| AC2  | GET    | `/api-mode/clawbacks`                                 | `ApiModeClawbackController_list_v1`             | `remediation:read`, K                            | v1 list (`api_clawbacks`), `?status&trigger&approvalStatus&from&to` (≤ 93 days, default 30), newest first; counts, approval and restore blocks                                                                                                                        |
| AC3  | GET    | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}`                    | `ApiModeClawbackController_detail_v1`           | `remediation:read`, K                            | + criteria (hashes truncated), signal facts, requester, submitter, the second person and reasons, hints `canSubmit`, `canApprove`, `canCancel`, `canRestore` for the caller                                                                                           |
| AC4  | GET    | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/targets`            | `ApiModeClawbackController_targets_v1`          | `remediation:read`, K                            | v1 list bound to the clawback, `?status&restoreStatus&mailboxId`; without `remediation:purge` the mailbox is masked and `headerFrom`/`subject` are `null`                                                                                                             |
| AC5  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/submit`             | `ApiModeClawbackController_submit_v1`           | `remediation:purge`, Idem, CAS                   | `{ mode: policy, verdict?, level? } \| { mode: explicit, action: move_quarantine \| move_junk \| soft_delete }` + `reason`, `expectedRowVersion` → `202 { id, status: queued \| pending_approval, requiresApproval, approvalReasons, approvalExpiresAt, rowVersion }` |
| AC6  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/submit-hard-delete` | `ApiModeClawbackController_submitHardDelete_v1` | `remediation:purge`, Idem, CAS                   | `{ reason, expectedRowVersion }`; `422 api_mode_hard_delete_disabled` while `API_MODE_HARD_DELETE_ENABLED` is off; always `pending_approval` (`hard_delete`); **portal only** (never an assistant tool)                                                               |
| AC7  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/approve`            | `ApiModeClawbackController_approve_v1`          | `remediation:purge`, Idem, CAS                   | `{ reason, expectedRowVersion }` → `200` the clawback (`queued`); `403 api_mode_purge_approval_forbidden` (`reason`: `api_key`, `missing_permission`, `requester`), `409 api_mode_purge_approval_not_pending` (`not_pending`, `expired`)                              |
| AC8  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/deny`               | `ApiModeClawbackController_deny_v1`             | `remediation:purge`, Idem, CAS                   | the same rules → `200` (`denied`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| AC9  | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/cancel`             | `ApiModeClawbackController_cancel_v1`           | `remediation:purge`, Idem                        | `{ reason }` → `202`; `409 api_mode_clawback_not_cancellable` (`status`)                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| AC10 | POST   | `/api-mode/clawbacks/{clawbackId}/restore`            | `ApiModeClawbackController_restore_v1`          | `remediation:purge` + `quarantine:release`, Idem | `{ reason }` → `202` (`restore.status: requested`); `409 api_mode_clawback_not_restorable` (`status`, `restoreStatus`)                                                                                                                                                |
| AU1  | GET    | `/api-mode/reports`                                   | `ApiModeReportsController_list_v1`              | `remediation:read`, K                            | v1 list (`api_user_reports`), `?status&assessment&analystVerdict&reporter&from&to` (≤ 93 days, default 30), newest first; `groupSize`                                                                                                                                 |
| AU2  | GET    | `/api-mode/reports/{reportId}`                        | `ApiModeReportsController_detail_v1`            | `remediation:read`, K                            | + reporter, report mailbox, the original's keys (hash truncated; sender and subject key untrusted), its detection, scan facts, analyst verdict and note, group members (≤ 50), triage history (audit, ≤ 50), feedback states                                          |
| AU3  | POST   | `/api-mode/reports/{reportId}/triage`                 | `ApiModeReportsController_triage_v1`            | `remediation:write`, Idem, CAS                   | `{ status: in_review \| resolved \| rejected, verdict?, note?, notifyReporter?, applyToGroup?, expectedRowVersion }` → `200 { report, groupResolved, feedbackQueued }`                                                                                                |
| AU4  | POST   | `/api-mode/reports/{reportId}/clawback`               | `ApiModeReportsController_clawback_v1`          | `remediation:purge`, Idem                        | `{ expand?, reason }` → `202 { reportId, clawbackId, status: searching, trigger: report \| campaign }`; `409 api_mode_report_original_unknown`                                                                                                                        |
| AU5  | GET    | `/api-mode/report-mailboxes`                          | `ApiModeReportsController_listMailboxes_v1`     | `remediation:read`, K                            | `{ data: [{ id, address, connectionId, provider, status, coverage, domain, reportMailbox, reportMailboxAt, lastReportAt }], max }`                                                                                                                                    |
| AU6  | POST   | `/api-mode/report-mailboxes`                          | `ApiModeReportsController_addMailbox_v1`        | `remediation:write`, Idem                        | `{ address, reason }` → `200 { mailbox, created, changed }`; `422 api_mode_report_address_out_of_scope`, `409 api_mode_report_mailbox_limit` (`max`)                                                                                                                  |
| AU7  | PUT    | `/api-mode/mailboxes/{mailboxId}/report-mailbox`      | `ApiModeReportsController_setMailbox_v1`        | `remediation:write`, Idem                        | `{ enabled, reason }` → `200 { mailbox, created: false, changed }`; `409 api_mode_report_mailbox_not_eligible` (`cause`), `409 api_mode_report_mailbox_limit`                                                                                                         |
| AS1  | GET    | `/api-mode/settings`                                  | `ApiModeSettingsController_get_v1`              | `remediation:read`, K                            | `{ settings, source: row \| defaults, rowVersion, updatedAt, defaults, bounds, platform: { hardDeleteAvailable, clawbackMaxWindowDays, purgeApprovalTtlHours, reportMailboxesMax } }`                                                                                 |
| AS2  | PUT    | `/api-mode/settings`                                  | `ApiModeSettingsController_put_v1`              | `remediation:write`, Idem, CAS                   | the eight settings + `reason`, `expectedRowVersion` (null = create) → `200` + `changed`, `created`; `409 api_mode_settings_conflict` (`currentRowVersion`); audited `api_mode.settings_org_updated` with from/to                                                      |

**Search (C2.3, C2.4, C4).** The api normalizes the input exactly as the copies were recorded: the Message-ID (with or without angle brackets) becomes the dedupe hash, the subject the explorer's subject key (sender and subject go together; the sender is lower-cased; a sender + subject window spans at most 7 days unless it comes from a campaign seed), a URL its identity SHA-256; attachment and URL hashes are hex SHA-256s (1–20 each). The window spans at most `API_MODE_CLAWBACK_MAX_WINDOW_DAYS` (31) days and starts within the 6-month copy retention; keys combine with OR; `directions` defaults to `inbound` and `internal`; report mailboxes are never searched. A `seedDetectionId` alone searches its Message-ID (the day before to 7 days after its receipt); `expand: { attachments?, urls?, senderSubjectHours? }` adds the seed's attachment hashes, URL hashes and sender + subject key within ± the hours (1–168): `trigger: campaign`. Rule failures are `422 api_mode_clawback_criteria_invalid` with `codes` only (`message_id_invalid`, `url_invalid`, `sender_subject_together`, `sender_invalid`, `subject_key_empty`, `window_required`, `window_too_long`, `window_before_retention`, `sender_subject_window_too_long`, `expand_needs_seed`, `seed_without_sender_subject`, `no_keys`, …); a foreign or unknown seed is `404 api_mode_detection_not_found`. The mailbox-worker materializes the hit list (`searching` → `ready`, or `no_match`, or `too_broad` above 20 000 copies); **what the targets list shows is what is purged** (frozen).

**Submit, approval and the second person (C5, C6).** A submit from `ready` or `review_required` with the version read; `policy` re-decides each target through the tenant's remediation policy with the chosen facts (a chosen verdict and level, default `critical`; else the signal's facts, the report's scan for a report clawback, else `phishing`), `explicit` applies the action. A purge whose eligible targets span more than the organization's `purgeApprovalMailboxes` (default 50) distinct mailboxes, any hard-delete purge and an automatic clawback held at its cap become `pending_approval` (TTL `API_MODE_PURGE_APPROVAL_TTL_HOURS`, 72) and request the alert `api_mode_purge_approval` (`notification.alert_requested.v1`, refs `clawbackId`, severity `medium`, `high` for hard delete; throttled per clawback through `notification_alert_windows`); otherwise `queued`. The approver must be a user holding `remediation:purge` who is neither the requester nor the submitter; the decider's permission-set hash is stored; an expired or decided request is `409 api_mode_purge_approval_not_pending`. A stale `expectedRowVersion` or a wrong state is `409 api_mode_clawback_state_conflict` (`status`, `currentRowVersion`). The api never queues a mailbox action itself: the mailbox-worker does (triggers `clawback` for automatic, `purge` for admin, report and campaign clawbacks). **Cancel** ends a `ready`, `review_required` or `pending_approval` clawback at once (a pending approval is cancelled with it) and asks the worker to cancel the pending actions of a `queued` or `running` one (running actions finish). **Restore** of a `completed`, `completed_with_errors` or `cancelled` clawback with succeeded targets restores them where the provider allows it (entries that need an approval and hard deletes are skipped with a code).

**Reports (C9–C12).** A report mailbox (≤ `API_MODE_REPORT_MAILBOXES_MAX`, 5) is an API-mode mailbox of an `api` or `hybrid` domain, `active`, `discovered` or `disabled` (shared mailboxes are often disabled accounts); an address not known yet is added as a `discovered` mailbox of its domain's connection. Its copies are never remediated or clawed back; each forward becomes a report the mailbox-worker parses and scans. Triage moves `pending_triage`/`in_review` reports to `in_review`, `resolved` (verdict required: `malicious`, `phishing_simulation`, `spam`, `clean`, `inconclusive`) or `rejected` (reason `analyst_rejected`); a resolved report can be reopened once (`409 api_mode_report_not_reopenable`); reports still being parsed and rejected reports are `409 api_mode_report_state_conflict`. `resolved` with `applyToGroup` (default true) resolves the open reports of the same original; with `notifyReporter` (default: `reportFeedbackEnabled`) one `mail.report.feedback_requested.v1` (`kind: resolved`) per resolved report is written to the outbox: the result e-mail never carries the reported message's subject or content. The analyst note is untrusted text and never in the audit row. A clawback from a report needs an identified original (Message-ID hash, else sender + subject key) and searches ± 72 hours (or `expand.senderSubjectHours`) around its date.

**Additive changes (C13, C14).** `GET /api-mode/overview` gains `internal: { copies24h, enabled }`, `clawback: { active, reviewRequired, pendingApproval, completed24h, targetsSucceeded24h, targetsFailed24h, oldestActiveAgeSeconds }` and `reports: { pendingTriage, inReview, received24h, resolved24h, oldestPendingAgeSeconds, reportMailboxes }`; each connection gains `internal`, `clawback: { targetsQueued24h, targetsSucceeded24h, targetsFailed24h }` (clawbacks are organization-wide; the connection block carries the target counters) and `reports`. Detections (list, detail and the mailbox's recent copies) gain `direction` (also on the list now), `verdictRevision` (late signals that raised the stored verdict) and `clawbacks` (≤ 5 public ids); the list takes `?direction=inbound|internal`. The remediation history accepts `trigger=clawback,purge` and its rows carry `clawbackId`.

```bash
# Search every copy of one Message-ID received in the last day, then preview the hit list.
curl -sS -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Idempotency-Key: $(uuidgen)" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' "$API/api/v1/api-mode/clawbacks" \
  -d '{"criteria":{"messageId":"<campaign-1234@fabrikam.example>","from":"<iso>","to":"<iso>"},"reason":"SOC campaign"}'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "$API/api/v1/api-mode/clawbacks/<clawback id>/targets?limit=50"
# Purge to Junk (a second administrator approves above the threshold).
curl -sS -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Idempotency-Key: $(uuidgen)" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' "$API/api/v1/api-mode/clawbacks/<clawback id>/submit" \
  -d '{"mode":"explicit","action":"move_junk","reason":"Remove the campaign","expectedRowVersion":2}'
# Resolve a report and its group, notifying the reporters.
curl -sS -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Idempotency-Key: $(uuidgen)" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' "$API/api/v1/api-mode/reports/<report id>/triage" \
  -d '{"status":"resolved","verdict":"malicious","expectedRowVersion":3}'
```

### AI assistant (`assistant` tag, steps AI-1, AI-2, AI-R and AI-6)

Chat-based management (design [28-ai-chat-management.md](../plan/steps/28-ai-chat-management.md) §4, ADR-0091–0094, [contracts §35](../architecture/contracts.md#35-ai-assistant-step-ai-1); full guide with examples: [docs/assistant/CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md](../assistant/CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md)). Every route is **user only** (API keys `403 user_required`), takes the tenant from the principal and answers `404` for another user's or organization's conversation, turn or execution. These routes are never chat tools themselves. The assistant's tools are the other `/api/v1` operations, called through a loopback with the caller's own token; their list per user is `GET /assistant/tools`, and the full mapping is the [capability matrix](../assistant/UI_TO_CHAT_CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md).

**Hardening and operations (step AI-6, [design 34](../plan/steps/34-ai-chat-hardening-operations.md), [ADR-0096](../architecture/adr/0096-assistant-async-report-jobs-session-job-token.md), [ADR-0097](../architecture/adr/0097-assistant-provider-routing-tenant-keys-pseudonymization.md), [contracts §35.6](../architecture/contracts.md#356-hardening-and-operations-step-ai-6)).** A20–A30 above (never chat tools). Changed views: settings gain `dataSharing` (`metadata` \| `pseudonymized`), `userDailyTokenBudget`, `useTenantKey`, `tenantKeyFallbackToPlatform`, `tenantKey`, `tenantKeysOffered`, `effectiveLimits` (the four editable fields are optional in `PUT`: absent = unchanged); status gains `keySource`, `fallbackActive`, `dataSharing`; assistant messages carry `feedback`; the report view gains status `queued` and `job`. A `report_generate` window longer than 7 days is queued (execution HTTP `202`, card `report.status: 'queued'`) and generated in the background; poll A19 until it is `available`. `assistant_budget_exhausted` gains `scope: 'user'` (top-level field of the error body, like `tenant`/`platform`). Examples and error codes: [CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md](../assistant/CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md#hardening-and-operations-step-ai-6). In Kubernetes these routes are served by the `api-assistant` Deployment through its own Ingress ([deployment](../deployment/assistant.md)); the URLs are unchanged.

**Reports (step AI-R, [design 31](../plan/steps/31-ai-chat-reports.md), [ADR-0095](../architecture/adr/0095-assistant-reports-native-tools-static-html-email.md), contracts §35.3).** The native tools `report_generate` and `report_send_email` are not operations: a turn calls them (A12), the report card (`kind: report`) arrives in `tool.completed`, the email proposal as `confirmation.required` and is confirmed with A17. New error codes: `assistant_report_not_found` 404, `assistant_report_purged` 410, `assistant_report_too_large` 413, `assistant_report_generation_limit` / `assistant_report_email_limit` 429, `assistant_report_storage_failed` 502, `assistant_report_sources_unavailable` 503; guard codes `report_recipient_not_allowed`, `report_needs_organization`. See [CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md](../assistant/CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md#reports-step-ai-r).

```bash
# Generate a report (fake provider locally), then read it back (JSON; the HTML is a string field).
curl -s -X POST "$API/api/v1/assistant/conversations/$CONVERSATION/turns" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"clientTurnId":"'"$(uuidgen | tr A-Z a-z)"'","message":"generate a security report"}' | jq '.executions[0].card.report.reportId'
curl -s "$API/api/v1/assistant/reports/$REPORT_ID" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" | jq '.report.status, (.html | length)'
```

**Event stream `assistant-stream/1`** (A12 with `Accept: text/event-stream`): `200 text/event-stream`, headers flushed with `turn.started` before the first model call; events `turn.started`, `text.delta`, `tool.started`, `tool.completed` (with the `assistant-card/1` result card; `status: pending_confirmation` for a proposed write), `confirmation.required` (AI-2: `{ confirmation, token }`, the only place the single-use token appears), `tool.denied`, then exactly one `turn.completed` or `turn.failed`; a `: heartbeat` comment every 15 s. Refusals before the stream (switches, budgets, rate limits, validation) are ordinary JSON errors. A disconnect cancels the turn. Proxies must not buffer the stream (the portal BFF sets `cache-control: no-store, no-transform` and `x-accel-buffering: no`).

```bash
# Ask over the event stream (fake provider locally; the directive calls exactly one tool).
curl -sN -X POST "$API/api/v1/assistant/conversations/$CONVERSATION/turns" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H 'Accept: text/event-stream' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"clientTurnId":"'"$(uuidgen | tr A-Z a-z)"'","message":"/tool policy_management_list {\"status\":\"active\"}"}'
# event: turn.started … event: tool.completed (card) … event: text.delta … event: turn.completed
```

**Confirmations (step AI-2,** [contracts §35.1](../architecture/contracts.md#351-confirmations-step-ai-2), [ADR-0093](../architecture/adr/0093-assistant-action-confirmation.md) amended**).** A write tool never runs inside a turn: the executor validates the call, reads the target's current state (the user's token), validates policy definitions through `POST /policies/validate`, stores a confirmation (server-authored summary `assistant-confirmation/1`, `actionDigest` over the canonical request, preconditions and identity, SHA-256 of a single-use token) and streams `confirmation.required`. The portal's confirmation card sends A17 with the token and the digest it showed; the api checks owner, session family, status, expiry, token, digest, tool, availability, re-authentication (`high` risk: password + TOTP unless the session re-authenticated in the last `ASSISTANT_REAUTH_WINDOW_SECONDS`), the turn lock, takes `pending → executing` atomically, re-reads the target (changed → `409 assistant_confirmation_state_changed`) and executes the stored request through its own pipeline with `Idempotency-Key` = the confirmation id. Codes: `404 assistant_confirmation_not_found`, `403 assistant_confirmation_session_mismatch`, `409 assistant_confirmation_not_pending`, `410 assistant_confirmation_expired`, `403 assistant_confirmation_token_invalid` (the fifth cancels), `409 assistant_confirmation_digest_mismatch`, `409 assistant_confirmation_tool_changed`, `409 assistant_confirmation_state_changed`, `401 assistant_reauth_required`, `401 assistant_reauth_failed` (`details.reason`), `409 assistant_turn_in_progress`. Audited as `assistant.confirmation_requested | executed | failed | cancelled | expired | refused`. Full guide: [CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md § Confirmations](../assistant/CHAT_API_DOCUMENTATION.md#confirmations-step-ai-2).

**Switches and errors.** `503 assistant_disabled` (platform switch `ASSISTANT_ENABLED` off), `403 assistant_not_enabled` (feature `ai_assistant` off; a platform operator enables it with `PUT /organizations/{id}/features/ai_assistant`), `403 assistant_tenant_disabled` (tenant setting off), `503 assistant_provider_unavailable` (breaker open), `503 assistant_busy`, `429 assistant_budget_exhausted` (`scope: tenant | platform`), `429 rate_limited` (6 turns per user per minute by default), `409 assistant_turn_in_progress`, `413 payload_too_large`. Turn failure codes in the stream and on the stored turn: `assistant_session_expired`, `assistant_turn_limit`, `assistant_refused`, `assistant_turn_abandoned`, `assistant_provider_error`. Each tool call is recorded with an executor decision (`allowed`, `denied_*`) and audited (`assistant.tool_executed` / `assistant.tool_denied`, correlation id shared with the operation's own audit rows).

## Conventions (step 6.1)

Binding decisions: [ADR-0050](../architecture/adr/0050-api-conventions.md) (resources, status codes, envelope, errors, Request-Id, strict validation, deprecation, OpenAPI), [ADR-0051](../architecture/adr/0051-pagination-cursor-filtering-sorting.md) (pagination, filters, sorting, scan budget), [ADR-0052](../architecture/adr/0052-rate-limiting-and-idempotency.md) (rate limits, idempotency), [ADR-0053](../architecture/adr/0053-public-internal-api-boundary.md) (public/internal boundary); design [docs/plan/steps/18-rest-api.md](../plan/steps/18-rest-api.md). `apps/api/test/route-inventory.e2e.test.ts` enforces them for every route.

- **Resources:** plural kebab-case nouns, at most two levels of nesting; commands are `POST /…/{id}/<verb>`.
- **Status codes:** `GET` 200; `POST` create 201 with the resource; synchronous command 200, asynchronous command 202; `PUT`/`PATCH` 200; `DELETE` 204 (a few older `DELETE`s answer 200).
- **Success bodies:** a single resource is returned bare; a collection is `{ "data": [...], "nextCursor": string | null }` (the three quarantine lists of step 5.3 keep `{ "items": [...], "nextCursor" }`).
- **Representation:** ids are strings (public UUIDv7 or decimal strings), timestamps ISO 8601 UTC with milliseconds, enums lower-snake; never message content, secrets or S3 keys.
- **OpenAPI extensions** for the portal: `x-eg-permissions`, `x-eg-rate-class`, `x-eg-idempotency`, `x-eg-cursor`, `x-eg-returns-secret`.

### Headers

| Header                                                          | Direction | Rule                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `X-Request-Id`                                                  | response  | Every response (success, error, unknown route, probes): a server-minted UUID, never taken from you |
| `x-correlation-id`                                              | both      | Yours if well formed (8–128 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`), otherwise a new one; echoed and audited   |
| `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining`, `RateLimit-Reset`     | response  | Every authenticated response: the most restrictive counter applied to the request                  |
| `Retry-After`                                                   | response  | `429`, `409 idempotency_request_in_progress`                                                       |
| `Idempotency-Key`                                               | request   | Required on the [sensitive mutations](#idempotency)                                                |
| `Idempotency-Replayed: true`                                    | response  | A stored response returned again                                                                   |
| `Deprecation: true`, `Link: <…#idempotency>; rel="deprecation"` | response  | A legacy sensitive route called without a key while `API_IDEMPOTENCY_LEGACY_MODE=warn`             |
| `Cache-Control: no-store`                                       | response  | Every `/api/v1` response unless the route sets its own                                             |

Quote `X-Request-Id` (or the body's `requestId`) when reporting a problem: it finds the request's log lines and its `audit_logs.metadata.requestId`.

## Errors

Every error of the api has one shape, rendered by the global `ApiExceptionFilter`:

```json
{
  "statusCode": 429,
  "error": "TOO_MANY_REQUESTS",
  "code": "rate_limited",
  "message": "Too many requests",
  "scope": "tenant",
  "correlationId": "…",
  "requestId": "0b9c6a2e-…"
}
```

`code` is a stable snake_case machine code (branch on it, not on `message`); `message` is a string, or a list of `"<path>: <issue>"` strings for validation errors (input values are never echoed); coded errors may add detail fields (ids, states, versions, limits; never content). Default codes:

| Status | Default `code`           | Specific codes and notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------ | ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `400`  | `validation_failed`      | `cursor_invalid`, `idempotency_key_required`, `idempotency_key_invalid`, `usage_period_unavailable`; malformed ids, unknown fields, invalid or used tokens                                                                                                                                    |
| `401`  | `unauthorized`           | Missing, invalid, expired or revoked credentials (`Invalid credentials` on login); user or organization no longer active; override ended                                                                                                                                                      |
| `403`  | `forbidden`              | `mfa_required`, `mfa_enrollment_required`, `user_required` (API key on a user-only route); module codes such as `quarantine_approval_forbidden`, `message_filter_forbidden` (`filter`, `permission`), `saved_views_member_only`; the reveal operations without `message:reveal` (`forbidden`) |
| `404`  | `not_found`              | `route_not_found` (unknown path); module codes (`message_not_found`, `suppression_not_found`, `saved_view_not_found`, …). **Resources of another tenant answer exactly like missing ones**                                                                                                    |
| `409`  | `conflict`               | `idempotency_request_in_progress` (+ `Retry-After`), `suppression_exists`, `suppression_limit_reached`, `saved_view_name_taken`, `saved_view_limit_reached` (`limit`), `saved_view_conflict` (`currentRowVersion`), duplicates, limits, state conflicts, version conflicts (`currentVersion`) |
| `413`  | `payload_too_large`      |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `415`  | `unsupported_media_type` |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `422`  | `unprocessable`          | `idempotency_key_reused`; `message_search_too_broad` (`maxMatches`); a refused destination server (`reason`, `field`); `Not implemented` recipient validation modes                                                                                                                           |
| `429`  | `rate_limited`           | `scope`: `principal`, `tenant`, `sensitive`, `expensive` or `route` (and module codes such as `quarantine_bulk_rate_limited`); `Retry-After` in seconds. The reveal limit (`API_MESSAGES_REVEAL_PER_MINUTE`) is `scope: route`                                                                |
| `500`  | `internal_error`         | Generic message only; details in the logs under `requestId` / `correlationId`                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `503`  | `service_unavailable`    | A dependency failed closed (rate-limit store, authentication store, domain-verification backend; since step 7.2 also the audit write of a reveal, which then returns no value); `inconsistent_read`, `report_timeout`, `message_search_timeout`                                               |

**Coded errors (step 3.3, 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.5).** The message lifecycle routes, the step 4.1 scan routes (`scan_engine_not_found`, `scan_settings_invalid` with `violations`, `version_conflict` with `currentVersion`, `organization_not_found`, `content_unavailable`, `scan_training_not_applicable`, `rspamd_unavailable`) and the step 4.2 platform routes (`hash_exists`, `threat_hash_not_found`, `rescan_job_not_found`, `rescan_job_not_cancellable` with `status`) and the step 4.4 BEC routes (`bec_executive_not_found`, `bec_vendor_not_found`, `bec_trusted_sender_not_found`, `bec_suppression_not_found`, `scan_not_found`, `bec_result_not_found`, `executive_exists` / `vendor_exists` / `trusted_sender_exists` with `id`, `version_conflict` with `currentVersion`, `bec_limit_reached` with `resource` and `limit`, `feedback_exists`, `vendor_domain_freemail` with `index`, `trusted_domain_freemail`, `feedback_codes_invalid` with `reasonCode`, `suppression_not_allowed` with `reason`, `inconsistent_read` (503)) and the step 4.5 DLP routes (`dlp_policy_not_found`, `dlp_rule_not_found`, `dlp_exception_not_found`, `dlp_pattern_not_found`, `dlp_dictionary_not_found`, `scan_not_found`, `dlp_evidence_not_found`, `dlp_policy_exists` / `dlp_rule_exists` / `dlp_exception_exists` / `dlp_pattern_exists` / `dlp_dictionary_exists` with `id`, `dlp_policy_in_use` / `dlp_rule_in_use` / `dlp_pattern_in_use` / `dlp_dictionary_in_use` with `references`, `version_conflict`, `dlp_limit_reached` with `resource` and `limit`, `dlp_reference_invalid` (400, `field`, `index`), `dlp_directions_invalid` (400), the D9 pattern codes `pattern_syntax` … `pattern_invalid_options` (400, `repetitionProduct`, `length`), `dictionary_terms_count` / `dictionary_term_invalid` (400, `index`), `evidence_purged` / `evidence_expired` (410), `evidence_unreadable` (500), `evidence_unavailable` and `inconsistent_read` (503); never a pattern, sample, term, address or value) carry their own stable `code` (since step 6.1 every other error carries a default one) (and, for `409`, the message's `currentState` and `currentVersion`); `message` stays human-readable:

```json
{
  "statusCode": 409,
  "error": "CONFLICT",
  "code": "message_version_conflict",
  "message": "The message changed since expectedVersion; read its lifecycle and retry",
  "currentState": "quarantined",
  "currentVersion": 6,
  "correlationId": "..."
}
```

### Validation errors

Request bodies, queries and path parameters are validated with zod schemas (the same schemas generate the OpenAPI shapes) before any database access. Since step 6.1 **every** body and query schema is strict at every object node: an unknown field is rejected (it used to be silently dropped on many routes; see the [changelog](#changelog)):

```json
{
  "statusCode": 400,
  "error": "BAD_REQUEST",
  "code": "validation_failed",
  "message": ["slug: is reserved", "colour: Unrecognized key"],
  "correlationId": "…",
  "requestId": "…"
}
```

## Pagination and lists

- `?limit=` 1–100 (default 25; quarantine search keeps its configured cap) and `?cursor=` (the opaque `nextCursor` of the previous page). Keyset order, newest first by default. The response is `{ "data": [...], "nextCursor": "…" | null }`; `null` means the last page.
- **No totals and no offsets** (counting is unbounded at a million messages a day; use the reports for aggregates).
- **Short pages.** Lists that filter on a non-indexed column examine at most `API_LIST_SCAN_BUDGET_ROWS` (5 000) index entries per request: a page may hold fewer rows than `limit`, even none, while `nextCursor` is non-null. **Follow `nextCursor` until it is `null`**; do not stop at an empty page.
- **Cursor v1** (`x-eg-cursor: v1`, every list added in 6.1): signed (HMAC) and bound to your organization, the resource, the filters, the sort and the first page's resolved time window, and valid for `API_CURSOR_TTL_SECONDS` (24 h). Repeat the same filters with it (you may omit `from`/`to`); anything else, a tampered or expired cursor or another tenant's cursor is `400 cursor_invalid` (restart the list without a cursor).
- **Cursor v0** (`x-eg-cursor: v0`, the lists of steps 1.2–5.3): unsigned, unchanged; an invalid one is a `400` (the quarantine search: `quarantine_cursor_invalid`). They never widen access: the tenant scope is always re-applied.
- Lists, details, usage and reports read the replica (ADR-0014): a row created a moment ago can appear after a short delay. Every create returns the created resource from the writer.

### Filtering and sorting

Each list documents its own allow-list of filters; anything else is `400 validation_failed`.

| Syntax                                                  | Meaning                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `field=value`                                           | equals                                                                                              |
| `field=a,b,c`                                           | one of (at most 10 values unless the list says fewer)                                               |
| `field.gte=` / `field.lte=` / `field.gt=` / `field.lt=` | typed range (numbers, timestamps)                                                                   |
| `field=true\|false`                                     | booleans                                                                                            |
| `from=` / `to=`                                         | ISO 8601 window `[from, to)`; required bounds on partitioned tables (defaults and maximum per list) |
| `sort=-receivedAt` / `sort=receivedAt`                  | descending (default) or ascending on the list's leading keyset key                                  |

Ids in filters (`domainId`, `messageId`) are resolved inside your organization: another tenant's id answers like a missing one (`404`).

**Which lists use this vocabulary.** Only the 9 lists added in step 6.1 (`x-eg-cursor: v1`: `/messages`, `/messages/{id}/recipients`, `/messages/{id}/attachments`, `/attachments`, `/messages/{id}/urls`, `/url-protection/clicks`, `/recipients/suppressions`, `/audit/logs`, `/platform/audit/logs`) take `sort` and the operators above. The 33 lists of steps 1.2–5.3 (`x-eg-cursor: v0`, e.g. `/users`, `/domains`, `/deliveries`, `/policies`, `/dlp/incidents`, `/quarantine/entries`, `/organizations`, `/api-keys`) take **no `sort` parameter** (a fixed keyset order, documented per operation) and keep their own module-specific filters as documented in their OpenAPI operations; unknown parameters are still `400 validation_failed`. Converging them is a follow-up (see below).

## Rate limits

Every authenticated request counts against fixed 60-second windows in Redis ([ADR-0052](../architecture/adr/0052-rate-limiting-and-idempotency.md)):

| Counter     | Applies to                                                                                        | Limit                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `principal` | each user or API key (override sessions count as the user)                                        | `API_RATE_PRINCIPAL_PER_MINUTE` (600)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `tenant`    | each organization (under an override: the target)                                                 | the organization's `api_requests_per_minute` (plan: 60 / 300 / 1 200 / 3 000; platform overrides possible), clamped to `[API_RATE_TENANT_MIN_PER_MINUTE, …_MAX_…]` (10, 10 000); `API_RATE_TENANT_DEFAULT_PER_MINUTE` (300) without a value                                                                                                     |
| `sensitive` | per principal, the [sensitive mutations](#idempotency) and secret-bearing routes                  | `API_RATE_SENSITIVE_PER_MINUTE` (30)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `expensive` | per principal: reports, `/billing/usage`, threat lookup, `/attachments`, policy test and dry-runs | `API_RATE_EXPENSIVE_PER_MINUTE` (60)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `route`     | route-specific limits (authentication, BEC feedback, DLP tests, domain checks, quarantine, …)     | per route, unchanged; since step 6.2 also `POST /webhooks/{id}/test` per webhook (`WEBHOOK_TEST_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE`, 6) and `POST /webhooks/{id}/replays` per organization (`WEBHOOK_REPLAY_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR`, 10 per hour); since step 7.2 the two reveal operations per principal (`API_MESSAGES_REVEAL_PER_MINUTE`, 30, one shared bucket) |

Every authenticated response carries `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` and `RateLimit-Reset` of the most restrictive counter. Over a limit: `429 rate_limited` with `scope` and `Retry-After`; wait that long. Public routes (login and the other **P** routes, `GET /meta`) keep their per-IP limits. When the rate-limit store is unreachable the api answers `503 service_unavailable` (fail closed). Operations: [runbook](../runbooks/api-rate-limits-and-reports.md).

## Idempotency

Send `Idempotency-Key: <1–128 chars of [A-Za-z0-9._:-]>` (unique per organization) on the sensitive mutations below. The first successful (2xx) response is stored for 24 hours; repeating the same request with the same key returns it again with `Idempotency-Replayed: true` and changes nothing. The same key with a different request (body, path, operation or caller) is `422 idempotency_key_reused`; while the first request is still running, `409 idempotency_request_in_progress` with `Retry-After`. Errors are not stored: fix the request and retry with the same key. Without the header: `400 idempotency_key_required`.

| Mechanism (`x-eg-idempotency`)                                                                                       | Routes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `required` — exactly once (effect, audit row and stored response in one transaction)                                 | `POST /messages/{id}/rescan`, `POST /messages/{id}/release`; quarantine `POST /quarantine/bulk`, `POST /quarantine/entries`, `POST /quarantine/entries/{id}/release`, `…/request-release`, `…/reject`, `…/delete`, `POST /quarantine/approvals/{id}/approve`, `…/deny`, `…/cancel`, `POST /quarantine/me/entries/{id}/release`, `…/request-release`; `POST /recipients/suppressions`, `DELETE /recipients/suppressions/{id}`; (step 6.2) `POST /webhooks`, `DELETE /webhooks/{id}`, `POST /webhooks/{id}/rotate-secret`, `POST /webhooks/{id}/deliveries/{deliveryId}/replay`, `POST /webhooks/{id}/replays`                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `legacy` — at most once per key (a reservation row before the handler, the response stored after it; since step 6.1) | `POST /domains/{id}/dkim/rotate`; `PATCH /organizations/{id}/status`, `POST /organizations/{id}/deletion`, `POST /organizations/{id}/deletion/confirm`; `POST /users/invitations`, `POST /users/{id}/invitation`, `POST /users/{id}/mfa/reset`; `POST /policies/{id}/versions/{n}/publish`, `…/activate`; `POST /risk-scoring/profile/versions/{id}/publish`, `…/activate`, `POST /risk-scoring/profile/reset`; `DELETE /bec/history`; `DELETE /dlp/evidence`, `DELETE /messages/{id}/scans/{scanId}/dlp-evidence`, `PUT` and `DELETE /dlp/compliance/aadhaar`; `POST /platform/dead-letters/replay`, `POST /platform/attachment-rescans`, `POST /platform/organizations/{orgId}/messages/{id}/scan-training`, `POST /platform/threat-hashes`, `POST /platform/threat-indicators` |

- The `legacy` routes accepted no key before step 6.1. While the deprecation switch `API_IDEMPOTENCY_LEGACY_MODE=warn` is set (default `enforce`), a keyless call is processed as before and answered with `Deprecation: true` and `Link: <…/docs/api/README.md#idempotency>; rel="deprecation"`. A `legacy` request whose process crashed between the effect and the stored response blocks the key for `API_IDEMPOTENCY_PENDING_TTL_SECONDS` (300 s); a retry after that may repeat the effect.
- **Secret-bearing responses are never stored**, so these routes take no key: `POST /api-keys`, `POST /smtp-connectors`, `POST /smtp-connectors/{id}/rotate-secret`, `POST /platform/overrides`, `POST /auth/mfa/totp/setup`, `POST /auth/mfa/recovery-codes` (`x-eg-returns-secret: true`, class `sensitive`) and the public authentication routes (single-use tokens, uniqueness and their rate limits protect them).
- **Exception (step 6.2, [ADR-0057](../architecture/adr/0057-idempotent-secret-bearing-routes-redacted-replay.md)):** `POST /webhooks` and `POST /webhooks/{id}/rotate-secret` return a signing secret **and** require an `Idempotency-Key` (`x-eg-secret-replay: redacted`). The stored response is the redacted representation (`secret: null`); a replay returns it with `Idempotency-Replayed: true` and never generates, re-encrypts or reveals a secret. A client that lost the original response rotates the secret with a new key.
- `POST /quarantine/entries/{id}/download-grants` (a single-use grant) and `PUT /quarantine/settings` (a replace with `expectedRowVersion`) take no key.

## Audit of sensitive reads

Every mutation writes an `audit_logs` row in its transaction. Since step 6.1 these **searches** are audited too, one row per search on its first page (without `cursor`): `message.searched` (when `sender`, `headerFrom`, `recipient`, `internetMessageId` or, since step 7.2, `subject` or `subjectPrefix` is used; subject values are hashed after normalization; domain and status filters are not identifying and not audited), `attachment.searched`, `audit.searched`, `platform.audit_searched` (with the target) and `threat_intel.looked_up`. The rows hold the filter names, the filter hash, SHA-256 hashes of identifying values and the result count; never addresses, subjects, hashes in the clear or content. Earlier audited reads are unchanged (quarantine search/preview/download, DLP evidence, BEC export). Detail reads (`GET /messages/{id}`, recipients, headers, attachments, URLs) are not audited: they are metadata behind `message:read` and, since step 7.2, return subjects, recipient addresses, matched users and To/Cc/Subject header values masked. **`message.revealed` (step 7.2):** every clear value of the message surface leaves the api through `POST /messages/reveal` (one row per revealed message, `metadata: { fields: ["subject"], bulk: true }`) or `POST /messages/{messageId}/reveal` (one row, `metadata: { fields, headerCount?, recipients?: { count, firstRcptSeq, lastRcptSeq } }`), resource `message` / `<messageId>`, committed on the writer before the response (an audit failure returns no value); never the values themselves. Every audit row carries `metadata.requestId`.

| Area                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Status                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Archive content (body) search, attachment content indexing, per-attachment downloads, backfill of mail received before journaling, PST/MBOX exports, per-case access lists, API-key export downloads | Not offered in 8.1 ([Archive](#archive-archive-tag-step-81)); content search with its own access design later (9–10)                                                                               |
| Account takeover: Microsoft Entra ID / Google Workspace adapters, provider-confirmed actions, provider integration management; a platform ATO view across tenants                                    | Step 8.5 (the API shows `availableInStep: "8.5"`); cross-tenant view not offered (metrics and alerts only)                                                                                         |
| Microsoft 365 / Google Workspace connectors                                                                                                                                                          | Step 8.2. **Integration surfaces today:** organization API keys (`/api-keys`), SMTP connectors (`/smtp-connectors`) and signed webhooks (`/webhooks`, step 6.2)                                    |
| Admin-portal screens for webhooks, delivery history and preferences                                                                                                                                  | Steps 7.x (the API is ready)                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Webhook events for SMTP-time refusals, per-recipient deliveries, URL clicks, DLP incidents; payload version 2; custom headers or mTLS; an egress proxy; SMS, chat or push alerts                     | Not offered in 6.2 ([Webhooks](#webhooks-webhooks-tag-step-62)); archive events 8.1, connector events 8.2                                                                                          |
| Deployment manager                                                                                                                                                                                   | Step 8.3                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `delivery_routes` management                                                                                                                                                                         | Not offered: no service reads the table (inbound destinations are `/domains/{id}/destinations`, outbound relay is SMTP connectors)                                                                 |
| Tenant threat indicators, IP/domain/URL reputation, brand lists                                                                                                                                      | Not offered: no engine reads tenant rows; brand lists are code constants. Use the lookup, BEC trusted senders, DLP exceptions and policy conditions                                                |
| Prices, invoices, payments, subscriptions                                                                                                                                                            | Not offered: no such data exists (`pricing: null`)                                                                                                                                                 |
| Total counts, offsets, field selection, ETags, bulk message export                                                                                                                                   | Not offered (unbounded at scale; field selection and ETags may come with the portal, 7.x). Step 7.2: an export of message metadata would need an async, audited, DLP-aware job; recorded follow-up |
| Masking of addresses inside URLs (URL analysis) and inside BEC evidence                                                                                                                              | Not offered (step 7.2): URLs are the analysis object, returned as the URL engine normalized them; BEC evidence addresses are sender side. Recorded follow-up                                       |
| Masking of attachment file names                                                                                                                                                                     | Not offered (open product-owner question, ADR-0062): file names stay in clear for `message:read` holders, as before step 7.2                                                                       |
| A body preview of messages that were never quarantined                                                                                                                                               | Not offered (open product-owner question, ADR-0062): message text only through the audited sanitized preview of a quarantined copy (step 5.3); `message:read_content` gates no route               |
| A clear-by-default read for privileged roles, a reveal token on later GETs, revealing SMTP texts or symbol options                                                                                   | Not offered (step 7.2, ADR-0062): clear values only through the audited `POST` reveal operations; free-text addresses stay masked                                                                  |
| Signing the v0 cursors                                                                                                                                                                               | Follow-up (14.1 or a 7.x need)                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `sort` and the shared filter operators on the 33 v0 lists                                                                                                                                            | Not offered in 6.1: fixed order and module-specific filters ([Filtering and sorting](#filtering-and-sorting)); follow-up with the portal (7.x)                                                     |

## Changelog

### Step API-3 (2026-11-27)

Additive: 19 operations under `api-mode` (clawbacks: create, list, detail, targets, submit, hard-delete submit, approve, deny, cancel, restore; reports: list, detail, triage, clawback; report mailboxes: list, add by address, set; settings get and put; 483 in total); the permission `remediation:purge`; the overview's `internal`, `clawback` and `reports` blocks (organization and per connection); `direction` (now also on the detection list), `verdictRevision`, `clawbacks` and the `direction` filter on detections; the remediation triggers `clawback`/`purge` (filter and view) and `clawbackId` on remediation rows; 15 coded errors (`api_mode_clawback_*`, `api_mode_purge_approval_*`, `api_mode_report_*`, `api_mode_settings_conflict`). No existing path, operationId or field changed.

### Step API-2 (2026-11-26)

Additive: 9 operations under `api-mode` (remediation policy get/put, domain override put/delete, remediation history list and detail, retry, remediate, restore; 464 in total); `remediationPaused` on the connection settings and views; the `remediation` block on the overview and connection views; `remediationState`, `remediationAction`, `remediationId`, `quarantineEntryId` and the `remediationState` filter on detections; `location` on every quarantine entry view and the `location` filter of `GET /quarantine/entries`; the quarantine origin `api_remediation`, the release statuses `restored`/`restore_failed` and the action types `mailbox_remediated`, `mailbox_restored`, `mailbox_restore_failed`, `mailbox_deleted` in the enums. No existing path, operationId or field changed; request and response shapes of the quarantine routes are unchanged apart from the added `location`.

### Step API-1 (2026-11-26)

Additive: 16 operations (14 under `api-mode`, 2 public `provider-notifications` callbacks), `protectionMode` on `POST /domains` and on the domain and organization views, `messages.apiCopiesToday` in the usage, the `remediation:write` gate of the `*.mailbox_remediation` grants, journal intake entitled by active API mode (the intake wizard step's optional `reason`), `mailbox_api` accepted as an archive merged-copy source. No existing path, operationId or field changed.

### Step AI-2 (2026-11-21)

3 new operations (421 in total) under the `assistant` tag ([AI assistant](#ai-assistant-assistant-tag-steps-ai-1-and-ai-2)): `GET /assistant/confirmations/{id}`, `POST …/confirm` and `POST …/cancel` (user only; confirm and cancel in the `sensitive` class). The turn stream gains the event `confirmation.required` and the execution status `pending_confirmation`; `ExecutionView` gains `confirmation`, the JSON turn result `confirmations`. No existing operation changed; confirmed writes reach the existing policy, quarantine, message, recipient-suppression and saved-view operations through the api's own pipeline (`Idempotency-Key` = confirmation id where the operation supports it).

### Step AI-1 (2026-11-20)

15 new operations (418 in total) under the `assistant` tag ([AI assistant](#ai-assistant-assistant-tag-steps-ai-1-and-ai-2)), all user only; two signed v1 lists (conversations, messages); the turn and "more" routes in the `expensive` class, the settings update in `sensitive`; one Server-Sent Events response (`assistant-stream/1`). No existing operation changed; `PermissionsGuard` now evaluates requirements through the shared predicate `satisfiesRequirement` (same decisions).

### Step 8.4 (2026-11-18)

34 new operations (354 in total) under the `ato` and `incidents` tags ([Account takeover](#account-takeover-ato-tag-step-84), [Security incidents](#security-incidents-incidents-tag-step-84)); 11 new signed v1 lists (33 in total); 14 new transactional Idempotency-Key operations (71 in total); the overview and the incident report are in the `expensive` class. Webhooks: four new public types (`security.incident.created`, `security.incident.updated`, `ato.account.risk_changed`, `ato.response.completed`; 14 in total; a subscription lists 1–14 types). Notification preferences: three new opt-out kinds (`ato_incident`, `ato_approval_requested`, `ato_digest`). Policy routes (WP3): ATO fields in the catalogue, `field_strict_only`, synthetic `ato` facts, `atoFactsSource` / `atoFacts` (no new route).

### Step 8.2 (2026-11-18)

24 new operations (378 in total since step 8.4) under the `provider-connections` tag ([Microsoft 365 and Google Workspace connections](#microsoft-365-and-google-workspace-connections-provider-connections-tag-step-82)); both lists are signed v1 lists; 12 new transactional Idempotency-Key operations (69 in total); two secret-bearing routes with redacted replay (the admin-consent URL and the probe address). Additive changes to existing routes: `smtp-connectors` `authType` gains `provider_relay` and connectors gain `providerConnectionId`; new codes `409 connector_managed_by_provider_connection`, `422 network_shared_provider` (smtp-connectors) and `422 domain_reserved` (`POST /domains`); the webhook event type `integration.health_changed` and the alert kind `integration_unhealthy`. New permissions `integration:read` and `integration:manage`.

### Step 8.1 (2026-11-17)

41 new operations (320 in total) under the `archive` and `ediscovery` tags ([Archive](#archive-archive-tag-step-81), [Legal holds and eDiscovery](#legal-holds-and-ediscovery-ediscovery-tag-step-81)): archive settings, retention policies, key versions and rotation, status, capture issues, the disposal log, export signing keys, masked metadata search (`X-EG-Index-Lag-Seconds`), detail, journal, frozen security results, the audited reveal, the sanitized preview, single-use re-authorized downloads, disposal of reviewed records; legal holds (organization-wide reads, release, creation inside a case), cases with custodians, worker-run case searches with completeness flags, hits and review tags, signed encrypted exports with download grants. Every list is a signed v1 list. 15 new transactional Idempotency-Key operations (57 in total). **Contract change of step 5.3 (quarantine):** `POST /quarantine/entries/{entryId}/delete` no longer answers `409 quarantine_retention_active` — the archive keeps its own encrypted copy, so archive retention no longer retains mail-bucket objects (the code stays in the catalogue, deprecated); a delete while a journal or policy archive capture of the message is still expected is allowed and the object purge is deferred (`archive_pending`, re-checked after one hour); legal holds are matched with the `archive-hold/1` criteria of case-scoped holds as well as the 5.3 subset. The policy catalogue's ARCHIVE text says "performed by the archive-worker, step 8.1".

### Step 7.3 (2026-11-16)

Additive only: 2 new operations (279 in total) under the `policies` tag — `POST /policies/validate` (`policy:manage`; the DSL check of a draft save plus the directory reference check on the replica; writes nothing) and `POST /policies/dry-run` (`policy:manage` and `message:read`; the sample dry-run over the newest stored evaluation per message of a bounded window, against a target compiled once; class `expensive` plus a per-principal limit; writes nothing). `GET /policies/catalog` gains required keys: per field `label`, `group`, `unit`, `suggestions`, `contexts`; at the top level `fieldGroups`, `operatorDefinitions`, `actionDefinitions`, `quantifiers`, `scopeNouns`, `contextRules`, `weekdays`, `timeZones`, `policyDirections`, `messageDirections`; and the `limits` keys `dryRunSampleMax` and `dryRunSamplesPerMinute` ([ADR-0063](../architecture/adr/0063-policy-builder-dsl-driven-no-client-evaluation.md); snapshot [policy-catalog.json](policy-catalog.json), drift-checked, with `timeZones` replaced by a marker because the list depends on the runtime's ICU build). New api configuration keys `POLICY_DRY_RUN_SAMPLE_MAX` (default 50), `POLICY_DRY_RUN_DEADLINE_MS` (default 10000) and `POLICY_SAMPLE_DRY_RUNS_PER_MINUTE` (default 6). No permission, existing field or operation changed.

### Step 7.2 (2026-11-14), part 2

**Contract tightening (pre-GA; no `/api/v2`): sensitive message metadata is masked for every caller** ([ADR-0062](../architecture/adr/0062-sensitive-message-metadata-masking-and-reveal.md)). Clients that read clear subjects, recipient addresses or matched users from these routes must call the new reveal operations with `message:reveal`:

1. `GET /messages` and `GET /messages/{messageId}`: `subject` is always `null`; new field `subjectMasked`.
2. `GET /messages/{messageId}/recipients`: `address` is masked (`f***@example.com`); new field `addressMasked`; `userId` is always `null`.
3. `GET /messages/{messageId}/deliveries` and `GET /deliveries`: `recipient` is masked; new field `recipientMasked`; addresses inside `finalSmtpResponse` are masked. `GET /messages/{messageId}/deliveries/events`: addresses inside `detail` are masked. `GET /messages/{messageId}/scans`: addresses inside Rspamd `symbols[].options` are masked.
4. `GET /quarantine/entries` and `GET /quarantine/entries/{entryId}`: `recipient` is masked for callers without a quarantine decision permission or `message:reveal` (read-only users, domain admins, platform admins under an override, API keys with `quarantine:read` only); new field `recipientMasked` on every entry (also `false` on the self routes).
5. (Added when the step was closed.) `GET /messages?recipient=` requires `message:reveal` (`403 message_filter_forbidden`, also when a saved view's definition uses it), and `GET /quarantine/entries?recipient=` is refused with the new code `403 quarantine_filter_forbidden` for callers who read recipients masked: an exact recipient search would confirm the masked address. `recipientDomain` is unchanged.

Additive (added when the step was closed): `GET /quarantine/entries` accepts `messageId` without `from`/`to` and then returns every entry of that message whenever it was quarantined (read by the message-led unique key; the range stays required without `messageId`).

Additive: 3 new operations (277 in total) under the `messages` tag — `GET /messages/{messageId}/headers` (`message:read`; the stored allow-list, safe subset in clear, To/Cc/Subject withheld, `availability`), `POST /messages/reveal` and `POST /messages/{messageId}/reveal` (`message:reveal`, audited `message.revealed` before the response, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `API_MESSAGES_REVEAL_PER_MINUTE`). New api configuration key `API_MESSAGES_REVEAL_PER_MINUTE` (default 30).

### Step 7.2 (2026-11-14), part 1

Additive: 5 new operations (274 in total) under the new `saved-views` tag — `GET /saved-views`, `POST /saved-views`, `GET /saved-views/{viewId}`, `PUT /saved-views/{viewId}` and `DELETE /saved-views/{viewId}` (`message:read`, users who are members only). New permission `message:reveal` (organization admins, security analysts, compliance auditors; super admins). Changed: `GET /messages` gains the filters `subject`, `subjectPrefix`, `senderDomain`, `recipientDomain`, `deliveryStatus` and `quarantineStatus`, reads its search drivers by index (exact pages without residual filters), matches `internetMessageId` with or without angle brackets, and has three new refusals (`403 message_filter_forbidden`, `422 message_search_too_broad`, `503 message_search_timeout`); its cursors are bound to the search driver, so cursors issued before this deploy answer `400 cursor_invalid` once. `GET /audit/logs` and `GET /platform/audit/logs` read a resource's history (`resourceType` + `resourceId`) by index (no contract change). The response items are unchanged in this part (masking follows in part 2).

### Step 7.1 (2026-11-13)

Additive only: 3 new operations (269 in total) under the `reports` tag — `GET /reports/delivery`, `GET /reports/processing` and `GET /reports/top-domains` (`report:read`, API keys accepted, class `expensive`, cached, `503 report_timeout`) — and the new field `outcomes` (verdict × action matrix) on `GET /reports/threats`. No existing field or operation changed. Internal only: the trusted client-IP forwarder headers of the admin portal ([Clients behind the admin portal](#clients-behind-the-admin-portal-internal)).

### Step 6.2 (2026-11-13)

Additive only: 17 new operations (266 in total) under the `webhooks` and `notifications` tags — webhook subscriptions with signed deliveries (`EG-Signature`, secret shown once, rotation with overlap), the event catalogue, the delivery history with attempts, single and range replays, test pings, security-alert settings and the personal preference center. New permission `webhook:read` (organization administrators, security analysts, compliance auditors); `webhook:manage` now gates the webhook mutations. `POST /webhooks` and `POST /webhooks/{id}/rotate-secret` are the two secret-bearing routes that take an `Idempotency-Key`, with a redacted replay (ADR-0057). No existing operation changed (the snapshot test compares every operationId).

### Step 6.1 (2026-11-12)

Two contract changes for existing clients (pre-GA; no `/api/v2`):

1. **Unknown request fields are rejected.** Every body and query schema is strict: a field the documented schema does not define is `400 validation_failed` (`"<path>: Unrecognized key"`) instead of being silently dropped. Nothing that was accepted changes meaning.
2. **`Idempotency-Key` is required** on the 22 `legacy` sensitive mutations listed under [Idempotency](#idempotency) (`400 idempotency_key_required` without it). `API_IDEMPOTENCY_LEGACY_MODE=warn` keeps the old keyless behaviour with `Deprecation` headers during the transition.

Additive: every error carries `code` and `requestId`; every response carries `X-Request-Id`, authenticated responses `RateLimit-*`; per-principal, per-tenant and class rate limits (`429 rate_limited` with `scope`); 20 new operations (messages, recipients, attachments, URLs and clicks, suppressions, audit logs, reports, usage, billing, threat lookup); the probes left the public OpenAPI document (they keep working); `/api/metrics`, `/api/health/*`, `/api/docs` are `404`.

## Readiness response

```json
{
  "service": "api",
  "status": "ok",
  "version": "0.1.0-local",
  "uptimeSeconds": 42,
  "checks": [
    { "name": "mysql", "status": "ok", "latencyMs": 2 },
    { "name": "mysql-reader", "status": "ok", "latencyMs": 2 },
    { "name": "redis", "status": "ok", "latencyMs": 1 },
    { "name": "s3", "status": "ok", "latencyMs": 6 }
  ]
}
```

Every Node service exposes the same two health endpoints on its own port (see the root README).
